    from functools import lru_cache
    
    from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict
    
    
    class Settings(BaseSettings):
        app_env: str = "development"
        database_url: str = "postgresql+psycopg://bridge:bridge@localhost:5432/bridge"
        supabase_url: str | None = None
        supabase_audience: str = "authenticated"
    
        model_config = SettingsConfigDict(
            env_file=".env",
            env_file_encoding="utf-8",
            extra="ignore",
        )
    
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=1)
    def get_settings() -> Settings:
        return Settings()
    from functools import lru_cache
    from typing import Any
    
    import jwt
    from fastapi import Depends, HTTPException, status
    from fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials, HTTPBearer
    from jwt import PyJWKClient
    from jwt.exceptions import PyJWKClientConnectionError, PyJWKClientError, PyJWTError
    
    from app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
    
    bearer_scheme = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)
    
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=4)
    def get_jwks_client(jwks_url: str) -> PyJWKClient:
        return PyJWKClient(jwks_url, lifespan=300, timeout=5)
    
    
    def get_current_user(
        credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials | None = Depends(bearer_scheme),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        if credentials is None or credentials.scheme.lower() != "bearer":
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Bearer token required",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            )
    
        if not settings.supabase_url:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Authentication is not configured",
            )
    
        base_url = settings.supabase_url.rstrip("/")
        issuer = f"{base_url}/auth/v1"
        jwks_url = f"{issuer}/.well-known/jwks.json"
    
        try:
            signing_key = get_jwks_client(jwks_url).get_signing_key_from_jwt(credentials.credentials)
            claims = jwt.decode(
                credentials.credentials,
                signing_key.key,
                algorithms=["ES256", "RS256"],
                audience=settings.supabase_audience,
                issuer=issuer,
                options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud", "sub", "role"]},
            )
        except PyJWKClientConnectionError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Authentication service unavailable",
            ) from exc
        except (PyJWKClientError, PyJWTError) as exc:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            ) from exc
    
        if (
            not isinstance(claims.get("sub"), str)
            or not claims["sub"]
            or claims.get("role") != "authenticated"
        ):
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            )
    
        return claims
    from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends
    from pydantic import BaseModel
    
    from app.api.dependencies import get_current_user
    
    router = APIRouter()
    
    
    class IdentityResponse(BaseModel):
        user_id: str
    
    
    @router.get("/me", response_model=IdentityResponse)
    def read_current_user(claims: dict = Depends(get_current_user)) -> IdentityResponse:
        return IdentityResponse(user_id=claims["sub"])
    from fastapi import APIRouter
    
    from app.api.v1.me import router as me_router
    
    router = APIRouter()
    router.include_router(me_router, tags=["identity"])
    from alembic import context
    from sqlalchemy import engine_from_config, pool
    
    from app.core.config import get_settings
    from app.db.base import Base
    
    config = context.config
    settings = get_settings()
    config.set_main_option("sqlalchemy.url", settings.database_url.replace("%", "%%"))
    target_metadata = Base.metadata
    
    
    def run_migrations_offline() -> None:
        context.configure(
            url=settings.database_url,
            target_metadata=target_metadata,
            literal_binds=True,
            dialect_opts={"paramstyle": "named"},
        )
        with context.begin_transaction():
            context.run_migrations()
    
    
    def run_migrations_online() -> None:
        connectable = engine_from_config(
            config.get_section(config.config_ini_section, {}),
            prefix="sqlalchemy.",
            poolclass=pool.NullPool,
        )
        with connectable.connect() as connection:
            context.configure(connection=connection, target_metadata=target_metadata)
            with context.begin_transaction():
                context.run_migrations()
    
    
    if context.is_offline_mode():
        run_migrations_offline()
    else:
        run_migrations_online()

    # Bridge Backend Engineering Handoff
    
    **Student talent marketplace | Python API service and screen-to-backend handoff**
    
    ## Purpose and scope
    
    This handoff translates the visible Bridge mobile prototype into backend work areas. The screens depict students presenting skills and portfolios, browsing paid tasks, submitting proposals, and communicating with task owners. Task owners can post work, review applicants, view talent profiles, manage matches, and exchange messages. The screens are evidence of intended interactions; endpoint names, persistence rules, and security behavior below are implementation recommendations unless explicitly marked as a selected technical decision.
    
    **Evidence boundary:** Screen descriptions reflect the Google Stitch project. Proposed behavior is identified as such where the UI does not define the rule. No payment flow, ratings calculation, verification workflow, or operational policy is confirmed by the screens.
    
    ## Selected backend stack
    
    | Layer | Selection | Responsibility |
    |---|---|---|
    | API service | Python with FastAPI | Separate, versioned HTTPS REST API between mobile clients and backend domain logic. |
    | Request models | Pydantic | Parse and validate API input and produce OpenAPI schemas. |
    | Database | PostgreSQL | Transactional persistence for profiles, tasks, applications, conversations, and messages. |
    | Database access | SQLAlchemy 2.x | ORM and transaction/session management. |
    | Schema migrations | Alembic | Versioned database schema changes. |
    | Authentication | Supabase Auth | Email/password and Google sign-in; clients send Supabase access JWTs to the API. |
    
    The mobile app calls FastAPI; it does not access marketplace tables directly. The API verifies the Supabase JWT signature, issuer, and expiry using Supabase's published signing keys (JWKS), then applies object-level authorization. Cache signing keys according to library/provider guidance and refresh them safely for key rotation. Do not implement JWT cryptography manually.
    
    Use SQLAlchemy transaction boundaries for multi-row state changes, and PostgreSQL constraints for uniqueness and referential integrity. If the API connects with a privileged database role that bypasses row-level security, RLS is not a substitute for API authorization. Use a least-privileged database role; treat RLS as defense in depth only when the request identity is safely propagated to the database.
    
    Keep one API service for the initial scope. Add a worker, queue, realtime transport, or additional service only when a confirmed product capability requires it. The separate API service requirement is fulfilled by the FastAPI boundary; the prototype does not establish a microservice topology.
    
    ## Screen inventory
    
    | Screen | Visible content and interaction | Backend implication |
    # Bridge Technical Requirements Document
    
    **Student talent marketplace | Version 1.1 | 2 November 2026**
    
    ## Purpose
    
    This TRD defines the selected backend technology baseline and technical requirements for Bridge's visible flows: profiles, task listings, proposals, applicant decisions, matches, and text conversations. The selected backend is a separate Python FastAPI service with PostgreSQL persistence. Product rules that the screens do not establish remain open and must be approved before implementation.
    
    ## System boundary
    
    ### In scope
    
    - Supabase Auth integration for the visible email/password and Google sign-in paths.
    - A separately deployed, versioned HTTPS API service that mediates all marketplace data access.
    - Bridge user mapping, user modes, profiles, and skills.
    - Task creation, publication/discovery, details, required skills, and ownership.
    - Proposal submission, applicant listing, and accept/reject transitions.
    - Match/status views and membership-protected text conversation history/send.
    - Summary values shown by the UI only after their source and calculation are defined.
    
    ### Out of scope until approved
    
    Payments/payouts, escrow, ratings algorithms, verification workflows, attachment storage, realtime delivery, recommendations, moderation operations, and administrative tooling.
    
    ## Selected technology architecture
    
    | Concern | Selected technology | Requirement |
    |---|---|---|
    | API service | Python + FastAPI | Own the backend API boundary; expose versioned JSON REST endpoints under `/v1`. |
    | Request validation and schemas | Pydantic | Define request/response models, constraints, and OpenAPI schemas at API boundaries. |
    # Bridge
    
    Bridge is a student talent marketplace concept. The product screens describe student profiles, task discovery and applications, task-owner applicant review, matches, and conversations.
    
    ## Project contents
    
    - [`backend/`](backend/) - separate Python FastAPI service with PostgreSQL, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, and Supabase Auth token verification.
    - [`docs/`](docs/) - product, business, technical, and engineering handoff documents.
    
    ## Start the backend
    
    See the [backend setup guide](backend/README.md) for prerequisites, local PostgreSQL setup, configuration, and run commands.
    
    The current backend is a foundation: it provides a public health route and a protected identity route. Marketplace workflows and domain tables have not been implemented yet.

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    # Resolve and ensure the working-tree directory SDD uses for one plan's
    # short-lived artifacts: task briefs, implementer reports, review packages,
    # and the progress ledger. Print the plan directory's absolute path.
    #
    # One directory per plan (.superpowers/sdd/<plan-basename>/) so a follow-up
    # plan in the same working tree can never read or overwrite another plan's
    # artifacts. A stale ledger misread as current progress makes controllers
    # skip whole task sequences — plan-scoping removes that failure structurally.
    #
    # Basename slugs collide when two plans share a filename (docs/alpha/plan.md
    # vs docs/beta/plan.md), so each workspace records its owning plan's path in
    # a plan-path marker (repo-relative in-repo, absolute outside). A workspace
    # owned by a different plan is skipped and the slug disambiguated with the
    # plan's parent-directory name, then a counter. A workspace with no marker
    # predates the marker scheme and is adopted for the current plan so in-flight
    # workspaces keep resolving — which means the first collision on such a
    # legacy workspace adopts instead of detecting; acceptable, marker-less
    # workspaces age out as plans finish.
    #
    # The workspace lives in the working tree (not under .git/) because Claude Code
    # treats .git/ as a protected path and denies agent writes there — which blocks
    # an implementer subagent from writing its report file. A self-ignoring
    # .gitignore at .superpowers/sdd/ keeps every plan's workspace out of
    # `git status` and out of accidental commits without modifying any tracked file.
    #
    # Single source of truth for the workspace location, so task-brief and
    # review-package cannot drift to different directories.
    #
    # Usage: sdd-workspace PLAN_FILE
    set -euo pipefail
    
    if [ $# -ne 1 ]; then
      echo "usage: sdd-workspace PLAN_FILE" >&2
      exit 2
    fi
    
    plan=$1
    [ -f "$plan" ] || { echo "no such plan file: $plan" >&2; exit 2; }
    
    slug=$(basename "$plan" .md)
    [ -n "$slug" ] && [ "$slug" != "." ] && [ "$slug" != ".." ] \
      || { echo "cannot derive a workspace name from: $plan" >&2; exit 2; }
    
    # Physical path, spelled the way pwd -P spells the plan path below: on Git
    # Bash, rev-parse prints C:/Users/... while pwd prints /c/Users/..., and the
    # repo-relative marker check needs both in the same form.
    root=$(CDPATH='' cd -- "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" && pwd -P)
    base="$root/.superpowers/sdd"
    
    # Normalize the plan path (physical directory, so relative/absolute/../
    # spellings of one plan compare equal) and express it as the marker value:
    # repo-relative when the plan lives under the repo root, absolute otherwise.
    plan_dir=$(CDPATH='' cd -- "$(dirname "$plan")" && pwd -P)
    plan_abs="$plan_dir/$(basename "$plan")"
    case "$plan_abs" in
      "$root"/*) plan_id=${plan_abs#"$root"/} ;;
      *)         plan_id=$plan_abs ;;
    esac
    
    # True when the workspace at $1 is (or becomes) this plan's: an existing
    # marker must name this plan; a missing marker means a new workspace or a
    # pre-marker legacy one, and either way the plan claims it by writing one.
    owns() {
      if [ -e "$1/plan-path" ]; then
        [ "$(cat "$1/plan-path")" = "$plan_id" ]
      else
        mkdir -p "$1"
        printf '%s\n' "$plan_id" > "$1/plan-path"
      fi
    }
    
    dir="$base/$slug"
    if ! owns "$dir"; then
      parent=$(basename "$plan_dir")
      dir="$base/$slug-$parent"
      if ! owns "$dir"; then
        n=2
        while ! owns "$base/$slug-$parent-$n"; do n=$((n + 1)); done
        dir="$base/$slug-$parent-$n"
      fi
    fi
    
    # Create-only: a repo may commit its own .gitignore here (e.g. to version
    # progress.md ledgers); overwriting it would revert that on every run.
    [ -e "$base/.gitignore" ] || printf '*\n' > "$base/.gitignore"
    CDPATH='' cd -- "$dir" && pwd

    import hashlib
    import secrets
    from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
    from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    
    import jwt
    from fastapi import HTTPException, status
    from jwt.exceptions import PyJWTError
    from sqlalchemy import select, update
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.core.config import Settings
    from app.db.models import RefreshSession, User
    
    ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(minutes=15)
    REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(days=30)
    JWT_ISSUER = "bridge-api"
    JWT_AUDIENCE = "bridge-api"
    PASSWORD_HASHER = None
    
    
    def signing_secret(settings: Settings) -> str:
        secret = settings.auth_jwt_secret
        if not secret or len(secret.encode("utf-8")) < 32:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Authentication is not configured",
            )
        return secret
    
    
    def hash_refresh_token(token: str) -> str:
        return hashlib.sha256(token.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    
    
    def issue_tokens(
        db: Session,
        user: User,
        settings: Settings,
        family_id: UUID | None = None,
    ) -> dict[str, str | int]:
        now = datetime.now(UTC)
        access_token = jwt.encode(
            {
                "sub": str(user.id),
                "iss": JWT_ISSUER,
                "aud": JWT_AUDIENCE,
                "iat": now,
                "exp": now + ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
                "jti": str(uuid4()),
                "token_type": "access",
            },
            signing_secret(settings),
            algorithm="HS256",
        )
        refresh_token = secrets.token_urlsafe(48)
        db.add(
            RefreshSession(
                user_id=user.id,
                family_id=family_id or uuid4(),
                token_hash=hash_refresh_token(refresh_token),
                expires_at=now + REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
            )
        )
        return {
            "access_token": access_token,
            "refresh_token": refresh_token,
            "token_type": "bearer",
            "expires_in": int(ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME.total_seconds()),
        }
    
    
    def decode_access_token(token: str, settings: Settings) -> UUID:
        try:
            claims = jwt.decode(
                token,
                signing_secret(settings),
                algorithms=["HS256"],
                issuer=JWT_ISSUER,
                audience=JWT_AUDIENCE,
                options={"require": ["exp", "iat", "iss", "aud", "sub", "jti", "token_type"]},
            )
            if claims.get("token_type") != "access":
                raise PyJWTError("Wrong token type")
            return UUID(claims["sub"])
        except HTTPException:
            raise
        except (PyJWTError, ValueError, TypeError, KeyError) as exc:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            ) from exc

## User

each user will have a role: admin, freelancer or student

## Assistant

I’ll add the role as a persisted account field and return it from `/v1/me`. Public registration and first-time Google sign-in will allow `student` or `freelancer` (default `student`); `admin` won’t be assignable through public auth endpoints, so users can’t grant themselves admin access. Existing accounts keep their role on later sign-ins.

Passwords are hashed with Argon2 using a maintained password-hashing library. Email is normalized and unique. The user row has a nullable password hash and nullable unique Google subject so either or both methods can identify one Bridge account. Each user has one role. Public registration and first-time Google sign-in may choose `student` or `freelancer` (default `student`); public endpoints cannot assign `admin`. Existing-account sign-in never changes a role. Admin assignment is provisioned outside public auth endpoints.

    from datetime import UTC, datetime
    from typing import Annotated, Literal
    
    from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, Response, status
    from google.auth import exceptions as google_exceptions
    from google.auth.transport.requests import Request as GoogleRequest
    from google.oauth2 import id_token as google_id_token
    from pydantic import AnyHttpUrl, BaseModel, EmailStr, Field, StringConstraints, model_validator
    from pwdlib import PasswordHash
    from sqlalchemy import select, update
    from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.api.dependencies import bearer_scheme
    from app.core.auth import (
        ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
        REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
        decode_access_token,
        hash_refresh_token,
        issue_tokens,
        signing_secret,
    )
    from app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
    from app.db.models import RefreshSession, StudentProfile, User
    from app.db.session import get_db
    
    router = APIRouter(prefix="/auth", tags=["authentication"])
    password_hasher = PasswordHash.recommended()
    dummy_password_hash = password_hasher.hash("bridge-invalid-login-placeholder")
    RoleChoice = Literal["student", "freelancer"]
    Skill = Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=1, max_length=60)]
    
    
    class StudentProfileInput(BaseModel):
        full_name: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=2, max_length=120)]
        key_skills: list[Skill] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20)
        experience_level: Literal["beginner", "intermediate", "advanced"]
        availability_hours_per_week: int = Field(ge=0, le=168)
        portfolio_url: AnyHttpUrl | None = None
    
        @model_validator(mode="after")
        def skills_are_unique(self):
            if len({skill.casefold() for skill in self.key_skills}) != len(self.key_skills):
                raise ValueError("key_skills must be unique")
            return self
    
    
    class RegisterRequest(BaseModel):
        email: EmailStr
        password: str = Field(min_length=8, max_length=128)
        role: RoleChoice = "student"
        student_profile: StudentProfileInput | None = None
    
        @model_validator(mode="after")
        def require_student_profile(self):
            if self.role == "student" and self.student_profile is None:
                raise ValueError("student_profile is required for student accounts")
            return self
    
    
    class LoginRequest(BaseModel):
        email: EmailStr
        password: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=128)
    
    
    class GoogleSignInRequest(BaseModel):
        id_token: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=8192)
        role: RoleChoice = "student"
        student_profile: StudentProfileInput | None = None
    
        @model_validator(mode="after")
        def require_student_profile(self):
            if self.role == "student" and self.student_profile is None:
                raise ValueError("student_profile is required for student accounts")
            return self
    
    
    class RefreshRequest(BaseModel):
        refresh_token: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=512)
    
    
    class TokenResponse(BaseModel):
        access_token: str
        refresh_token: str
        token_type: Literal["bearer"] = "bearer"
        expires_in: int
    
    
    class IdentityResponse(BaseModel):
        user_id: str
        role: Literal["admin", "freelancer", "student"]
    
    
    def normalize_email(email: str) -> str:
        return email.strip().casefold()
    
    
    def add_student_profile(db: Session, user: User, profile: StudentProfileInput | None) -> None:
        if profile is not None:
            db.add(
                StudentProfile(
                    user_id=user.id,
                    full_name=profile.full_name,
                    key_skills=profile.key_skills,
                    experience_level=profile.experience_level,
                    availability_hours_per_week=profile.availability_hours_per_week,
                    portfolio_url=str(profile.portfolio_url) if profile.portfolio_url else None,
                )
            )
    
    
    def unauthorized() -> HTTPException:
        return HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
            detail="Invalid credentials",
            headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
        )
    
    
    def configured_google_audiences(settings: Settings) -> list[str]:
        audiences = [value.strip() for value in settings.google_client_ids if value.strip()]
        if not audiences:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Google sign-in is not configured",
            )
        return audiences
    
    
    def verify_google_token(token: str, settings: Settings) -> dict:
        audiences = configured_google_audiences(settings)
        try:
            claims = google_id_token.verify_oauth2_token(
                token,
                GoogleRequest(),
                audience=audiences[0] if len(audiences) == 1 else audiences,
            )
        except ValueError as exc:
            raise unauthorized() from exc
        except google_exceptions.GoogleAuthError as exc:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Google authentication service unavailable",
            ) from exc
        if (
            not isinstance(claims.get("sub"), str)
            or not claims["sub"]
            or not isinstance(claims.get("email"), str)
            or claims.get("email_verified") is not True
        ):
            raise unauthorized()
        return claims
    
    
    @router.post("/register", response_model=TokenResponse, status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED)
    def register(
        request: RegisterRequest,
        db: Session = Depends(get_db),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    ) -> dict:
        signing_secret(settings)
        email = normalize_email(str(request.email))
        if db.scalar(select(User.id).where(User.email == email)) is not None:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists")
    
        user = User(
            email=email,
            password_hash=password_hasher.hash(request.password),
            role=request.role,
        )
        db.add(user)
        try:
            db.flush()
            add_student_profile(db, user, request.student_profile)
            tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings)
            db.commit()
        except IntegrityError as exc:
            db.rollback()
            raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists") from exc
        return tokens
    
    
    @router.post("/login", response_model=TokenResponse)
    def login(
        request: LoginRequest,
        db: Session = Depends(get_db),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    ) -> dict:
        signing_secret(settings)
        user = db.scalar(select(User).where(User.email == normalize_email(str(request.email))))
        encoded_password = user.password_hash if user and user.password_hash else dummy_password_hash
        if not password_hasher.verify(request.password, encoded_password) or user is None:
            raise unauthorized()
        tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings)
        db.commit()
        return tokens
    
    
    @router.post("/google", response_model=TokenResponse)
    def google_sign_in(
        request: GoogleSignInRequest,
        db: Session = Depends(get_db),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    ) -> dict:
        signing_secret(settings)
        claims = verify_google_token(request.id_token, settings)
        email = normalize_email(claims["email"])
        user = db.scalar(select(User).where(User.google_sub == claims["sub"]))
        if user is not None:
            if user.email != email:
                existing_email = db.scalar(select(User.id).where(User.email == email))
                if existing_email is not None and existing_email != user.id:
                    raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists")
                user.email = email
        else:
            user = db.scalar(select(User).where(User.email == email))
            if user is not None and user.google_sub not in (None, claims["sub"]):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists")
            if user is None:
                user = User(email=email, google_sub=claims["sub"], role=request.role)
                db.add(user)
                try:
                    db.flush()
                    add_student_profile(db, user, request.student_profile)
                except IntegrityError as exc:
                    db.rollback()
                    raise HTTPException(
                        status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT,
                        detail="Account already exists",
                    ) from exc
            else:
                user.google_sub = claims["sub"]
    
        tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings)
        db.commit()
        return tokens
    
    
    @router.post("/refresh", response_model=TokenResponse)
    def refresh(
        request: RefreshRequest,
        db: Session = Depends(get_db),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    ) -> dict:
        signing_secret(settings)
        session = db.scalar(
            select(RefreshSession)
            .where(RefreshSession.token_hash == hash_refresh_token(request.refresh_token))
            .with_for_update()
        )
        if session is None:
            raise unauthorized()
    
        now = datetime.now(UTC)
        if session.revoked_at is not None:
            db.execute(
                update(RefreshSession)
                .where(
                    RefreshSession.family_id == session.family_id,
                    RefreshSession.revoked_at.is_(None),
                )
                .values(revoked_at=now)
            )
            db.commit()
            raise unauthorized()
        if session.expires_at <= now:
            session.revoked_at = now
            db.commit()
            raise unauthorized()
    
        user = db.get(User, session.user_id)
        if user is None:
            session.revoked_at = now
            db.commit()
            raise unauthorized()
    
        family_id = session.family_id
        session.revoked_at = now
        tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings, family_id=family_id)
        db.commit()
        return tokens
    
    
    @router.post("/logout", status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)
    def logout(request: RefreshRequest, db: Session = Depends(get_db)) -> Response:
        db.execute(
            update(RefreshSession)
            .where(
                RefreshSession.token_hash == hash_refresh_token(request.refresh_token),
                RefreshSession.revoked_at.is_(None),
            )
            .values(revoked_at=datetime.now(UTC))
        )
        db.commit()
        return Response(status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)
    
    
    @router.get("/me", response_model=IdentityResponse, include_in_schema=False)
    def read_current_user(
        user: User = Depends(bearer_scheme),
    ) -> IdentityResponse:
        # The public identity route is registered in me.py; keep this path unused.
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND)

    from datetime import UTC, datetime
    from typing import Annotated, Literal
    
    from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, Response, status
    from google.auth import exceptions as google_exceptions
    from google.auth.transport.requests import Request as GoogleRequest
    from google.oauth2 import id_token as google_id_token
    from pydantic import AnyHttpUrl, BaseModel, EmailStr, Field, StringConstraints, model_validator
    from pwdlib import PasswordHash
    from sqlalchemy import select, update
    from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.core.auth import (
        decode_access_token,
        hash_refresh_token,
        issue_tokens,
        signing_secret,
    )
    from app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
    from app.db.models import RefreshSession, StudentProfile, User
    from app.db.session import get_db
    
    router = APIRouter(prefix="/auth", tags=["authentication"])
    password_hasher = PasswordHash.recommended()
    dummy_password_hash = password_hasher.hash("bridge-invalid-login-placeholder")
    RoleChoice = Literal["student", "freelancer"]
    Skill = Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=1, max_length=60)]
    
    
    class StudentProfileInput(BaseModel):
        full_name: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=2, max_length=120)]
        key_skills: list[Skill] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20)
        experience_level: Literal["beginner", "intermediate", "advanced"]
        availability_hours_per_week: int = Field(ge=0, le=168)
        portfolio_url: AnyHttpUrl | None = None
    
        @model_validator(mode="after")
        def skills_are_unique(self):
            if len({skill.casefold() for skill in self.key_skills}) != len(self.key_skills):
                raise ValueError("key_skills must be unique")
            return self
    
    
    class RegisterRequest(BaseModel):
        email: EmailStr
        password: str = Field(min_length=8, max_length=128)
        role: RoleChoice = "student"
        student_profile: StudentProfileInput | None = None
    
        @model_validator(mode="after")
        def require_student_profile(self):
            if self.role == "student" and self.student_profile is None:
                raise ValueError("student_profile is required for student accounts")
            if self.role == "freelancer" and self.student_profile is not None:
                raise ValueError("student_profile is only valid for student accounts")
            return self
    
    
    class LoginRequest(BaseModel):
        email: EmailStr
        password: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=128)
    
    
    class GoogleSignInRequest(BaseModel):
        id_token: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=8192)
        role: RoleChoice = "student"
        student_profile: StudentProfileInput | None = None
    
    class RefreshRequest(BaseModel):
        refresh_token: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=512)
    
    
    class TokenResponse(BaseModel):
        access_token: str
        refresh_token: str
        token_type: Literal["bearer"] = "bearer"
        expires_in: int
    
    
    class IdentityResponse(BaseModel):
        user_id: str
        role: Literal["admin", "freelancer", "student"]
    
    
    def normalize_email(email: str) -> str:
        return email.strip().casefold()
    
    
    def add_student_profile(db: Session, user: User, profile: StudentProfileInput | None) -> None:
        if profile is not None:
            db.add(
                StudentProfile(
                    user_id=user.id,
                    full_name=profile.full_name,
                    key_skills=profile.key_skills,

                    availability_hours_per_week=profile.availability_hours_per_week,
                    portfolio_url=str(profile.portfolio_url) if profile.portfolio_url else None,
                )
            )

        ):

        return claims

            )
            db.commit()
            raise unauthorized()
        if session.expires_at <= now:
            session.revoked_at = now
            db.commit()
            raise unauthorized()
    
        user = db.get(User, session.user_id)
        if user is None:
            session.revoked_at = now
            db.commit()
            raise unauthorized()
    
        family_id = session.family_id
        session.revoked_at = now
        tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings, family_id=family_id)
        db.commit()
        return tokens
    
    
    @router.post("/logout", status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)
    def logout(request: RefreshRequest, db: Session = Depends(get_db)) -> Response:
        db.execute(
            update(RefreshSession)
            .where(
                RefreshSession.token_hash == hash_refresh_token(request.refresh_token),
                RefreshSession.revoked_at.is_(None),
            )
            .values(revoked_at=datetime.now(UTC))
        )
        db.commit()
        return Response(status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)
    import hashlib
    import secrets
    from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
    from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    
    import jwt
    from fastapi import HTTPException, status
    from jwt.exceptions import PyJWTError
    from sqlalchemy import select, update
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.core.config import Settings
    from app.db.models import RefreshSession, User
    
    ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(minutes=15)
    REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(days=30)
    JWT_ISSUER = "bridge-api"
    JWT_AUDIENCE = "bridge-api"
    def signing_secret(settings: Settings) -> str:
        secret = settings.auth_jwt_secret
        if not secret or len(secret.encode("utf-8")) < 32:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Authentication is not configured",
            )
        return secret
    
    
    def hash_refresh_token(token: str) -> str:
        return hashlib.sha256(token.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    
    
    def issue_tokens(
        db: Session,
        user: User,
        settings: Settings,
        family_id: UUID | None = None,
    ) -> dict[str, str | int]:
        now = datetime.now(UTC)
        access_token = jwt.encode(
            {
                "sub": str(user.id),
                "iss": JWT_ISSUER,
                "aud": JWT_AUDIENCE,
                "iat": now,
                "exp": now + ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
                "jti": str(uuid4()),
                "token_type": "access",
            },
            signing_secret(settings),
            algorithm="HS256",
        )
        refresh_token = secrets.token_urlsafe(48)
        db.add(
            RefreshSession(
                user_id=user.id,
                family_id=family_id or uuid4(),
                token_hash=hash_refresh_token(refresh_token),
                expires_at=now + REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
            )
        )
        return {
            "access_token": access_token,
            "refresh_token": refresh_token,
            "token_type": "bearer",
            "expires_in": int(ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME.total_seconds()),
        }
    
    
    def decode_access_token(token: str, settings: Settings) -> UUID:
        try:
            claims = jwt.decode(
                token,
                signing_secret(settings),
                algorithms=["HS256"],
                issuer=JWT_ISSUER,
                audience=JWT_AUDIENCE,
                options={"require": ["exp", "iat", "iss", "aud", "sub", "jti", "token_type"]},
            )
            if claims.get("token_type") != "access":
                raise PyJWTError("Wrong token type")
            return UUID(claims["sub"])
        except HTTPException:
            raise
        except (PyJWTError, ValueError, TypeError, KeyError) as exc:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            ) from exc
    from datetime import UTC, datetime
    from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    
    from sqlalchemy import JSON, CheckConstraint, DateTime, ForeignKey, String, Text, Uuid, func
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped, mapped_column
    
    from app.db.base import Base
    
    
    class User(Base):
        __tablename__ = "users"
        __table_args__ = (CheckConstraint("role IN ('admin', 'freelancer', 'student')", name="ck_users_role"),)
    
        id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid4)
        email: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(320), unique=True)
        password_hash: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(Text, nullable=True)
        google_sub: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(255), unique=True, nullable=True)
        role: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20), default="student", server_default="student")
        created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
            DateTime(timezone=True), default=lambda: datetime.now(UTC), server_default=func.now()
        )
    
    
    class StudentProfile(Base):
        __tablename__ = "student_profiles"
        __table_args__ = (
            CheckConstraint(
                "experience_level IN ('beginner', 'intermediate', 'advanced')",
                name="ck_student_profiles_experience_level",
            ),
            CheckConstraint(
                "availability_hours_per_week BETWEEN 0 AND 168",
                name="ck_student_profiles_availability",
            ),
        )
    
        user_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(
            Uuid, ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), primary_key=True
        )
        full_name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(120))
        key_skills: Mapped[list[str]] = mapped_column(JSON)
        experience_level: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20))
        availability_hours_per_week: Mapped[int] = mapped_column()
        portfolio_url: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(2048), nullable=True)
    
    
    class RefreshSession(Base):
        __tablename__ = "refresh_sessions"
    
        id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid4)
        user_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(
            Uuid, ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), index=True
        )
        family_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, index=True)
        token_hash: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), unique=True)
        expires_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
        revoked_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=True)
        created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
            DateTime(timezone=True), default=lambda: datetime.now(UTC), server_default=func.now()
        )
    from fastapi import Depends, HTTPException, status
    from fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials, HTTPBearer
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.core.auth import decode_access_token
    from app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
    from app.db.models import User
    from app.db.session import get_db
    
    bearer_scheme = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)
    
    
    def get_current_user(
        credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials | None = Depends(bearer_scheme),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
        db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    ) -> User:
        if credentials is None or credentials.scheme.lower() != "bearer":
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Bearer token required",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            )
    
        user_id = decode_access_token(credentials.credentials, settings)
        user = db.get(User, user_id)
        if user is None:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            )
        return user
    from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends
    from pydantic import BaseModel
    
    from app.api.dependencies import get_current_user
    from app.db.models import User
    
    router = APIRouter()
    
    
    class IdentityResponse(BaseModel):
        user_id: str
        role: str
    
    
    @router.get("/me", response_model=IdentityResponse)
    def read_current_user(user: User = Depends(get_current_user)) -> IdentityResponse:
        return IdentityResponse(user_id=str(user.id), role=user.role)
    from fastapi import APIRouter
    
    from app.api.v1.auth import router as auth_router
    from app.api.v1.me import router as me_router
    
    router = APIRouter()
    router.include_router(auth_router)
    router.include_router(me_router, tags=["identity"])

    # Bridge-owned authentication design
    
    ## Intent
    
    Replace the backend's Supabase JWT verification with Bridge-owned account and session APIs. Google remains an identity provider for users who choose Google sign-in; Supabase is removed from runtime authentication.
    
    ## Goals
    
    - Support email/password registration and login.
    - Accept a Google ID token from the client, verify it server-side, and sign the user into a Bridge account.
    - Issue Bridge access tokens and rotating refresh tokens for both sign-in methods.
    - Protect `GET /v1/me` using Bridge access tokens.
    - Persist users and refresh sessions in the existing PostgreSQL database.
    - Store one role per user: `admin`, `freelancer`, or `student`; expose it through `/v1/me`.
    - Keep existing protected routes compatible with an authenticated identity subject where practical.
    
    ## Non-goals
    
    - Google browser redirect/callback handling in the backend.
    - Email verification, password reset, MFA, account deletion, or admin tooling.
    - Client implementation (this repository contains only the backend).
    - Marketplace profile or role creation.
    
    ## Approaches
    
    1. **Client Google ID token exchange, Bridge access JWT plus stored rotating refresh tokens (selected).** Fits a mobile client and the existing bearer-token API. Google credentials are validated by Bridge; Bridge owns subsequent API sessions and can revoke refresh sessions.
    2. **Backend Google redirect/callback flow.** Centralizes the browser flow, but changes the API into a browser-oriented OAuth client and is less suited to the described mobile client.
    3. **Database-backed session on every request.** Simple revocation, but adds a database lookup to every protected request. A short-lived signed access token plus persisted refresh sessions gives a smaller runtime dependency while retaining refresh revocation.
    
    ## API and behavior
    
    - `POST /v1/auth/register`: accept email and password, create an account, and return a Bridge token pair.
    - `POST /v1/auth/login`: verify email and password and return a Bridge token pair. Use a generic invalid-credentials response.
    - `POST /v1/auth/google`: accept a Google ID token, verify signature, issuer, expiry, and configured audience using Google's maintained verification library; require a verified email. Find or create the account and return a Bridge token pair. A verified Google email matching an existing account maps to that account; a Google subject already linked to another account is rejected.
    - `POST /v1/auth/refresh`: accept a refresh token, rotate it atomically, revoke the prior token, and return a new token pair. Reuse of a rotated token revokes its token family.
    - `POST /v1/auth/logout`: revoke the presented refresh token/session; access tokens expire on their short lifetime.
    - `GET /v1/me`: return the authenticated Bridge user ID using the Bridge access token.
    
    Access tokens are short-lived signed JWTs. Refresh tokens are random opaque values; only their hashes are stored in PostgreSQL. Passwords are hashed with Argon2 using a maintained password-hashing library. Email is normalized and unique. The user row has a nullable password hash and nullable unique Google subject so either or both methods can identify one Bridge account. Each user has one role. Public registration and first-time Google sign-in may choose `student` or `freelancer` (default `student`); public endpoints cannot assign `admin`. Existing-account sign-in never changes a role. Admin assignment is provisioned outside public auth endpoints.
    
    ## Data and configuration
    
    Add a users table and refresh-session table through an Alembic migration. User IDs are UUIDs. Sessions store token hash, user ID, family ID, expiry, revocation, and creation timestamps. Keep database transactions around account creation and refresh rotation.
    
    Remove `SUPABASE_URL` and Supabase audience settings. Add a required Bridge JWT signing secret and configured Google OAuth client ID(s) as server settings. Do not provide production signing-secret defaults; document local setup and fail closed when auth configuration is missing. No Google client secret is needed for ID-token verification.
    
    ## Security and compatibility
    
    - Bound and validate credentials; never log passwords, tokens, or Google ID tokens.
    - Return generic login failures and reject unverified Google email claims.
    - Verify Google ID tokens with a maintained library and configured audience; never trust decoded claims without signature verification.
    - Require bearer authentication on `/v1/me`; use the Bridge user UUID as JWT subject and response identity.
    - The current repository has no user table or shipped client, so existing Supabase accounts are not migrated automatically. Client auth integration and account recovery remain follow-up work.
    
    ## Acceptance
    
    - Supabase settings and remote JWKS verification are no longer required for protected Bridge routes.
    - Email/password registration and login, Google ID token exchange, refresh rotation, and logout have documented request/response contracts and correct persistence behavior.
    - `/v1/me` accepts only valid Bridge access tokens and identifies the Bridge user.
    - Database schema can be created and rolled back through Alembic.
    - Backend README and auth handoff documentation describe the new configuration and flow.
    
    ## Builder decisions
    
    - Use a maintained Argon2 password-hashing library and Google's official ID-token verification library.
    - Use UUID identities, opaque refresh tokens hashed at rest, and short-lived JWT access tokens.
    - Do not add verification or recovery flows without product requirements.
    # Bridge-owned Authentication Implementation Plan
    
    > **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
    
    **Goal:** Replace Supabase token verification with Bridge-owned email/password and Google sign-in APIs backed by PostgreSQL.
    
    **Architecture:** Add Bridge user and refresh-session tables, issue short-lived Bridge JWT access tokens, and store only hashes of rotating opaque refresh tokens. Google clients exchange a Google ID token at the API; the API verifies it and issues the same Bridge tokens.
    
    **Tech Stack:** FastAPI, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, PostgreSQL, PyJWT, Argon2 password hashing, Google ID token verification.
    
    **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-bridge-owned-auth-design.md`
    
    ## Global Constraints
    
    - Never store plaintext passwords or refresh tokens.
    - Do not trust decoded Google claims without signature verification and configured audience validation.
    - Keep `/health` independent of database and auth configuration.
    - Do not add password recovery, email verification, MFA, or client implementation.
    
    ## Review Focus
    
    - Missing or short Bridge signing secret: auth operations fail closed with a clear server configuration error.
    - Malformed, expired, wrong-audience, or unverified-email Google ID token: Google exchange is rejected.
    - Duplicate normalized emails or Google subjects: do not create or cross-link duplicate accounts.
    - Refresh token rotation race or replay: only one rotation succeeds and reuse revokes the session family.
    - Existing Supabase token: protected routes reject it after the provider verifier is removed.
    
    ---

    - `RefreshSession`: UUID `id`, `user_id`, `family_id`, unique token hash, expiry, optional revoked timestamp, creation timestamp.
    - Settings: optional `auth_jwt_secret` and `google_client_ids`; auth calls validate required values at use time so health remains independent.

    ### Task 2: Implement Bridge token verification and auth routes
    
    **Files:**

    **Interfaces:**
    - `get_current_user(...) -> User`: validate Bridge bearer JWT and resolve its UUID subject to an active database user.
    - Auth routes: `POST /auth/register`, `/auth/login`, `/auth/google`, `/auth/refresh`, `/auth/logout`.
    - Token response: `access_token`, `refresh_token`, `token_type="bearer"`, `expires_in`.
    - Registration and first Google sign-in accept `role=student|freelancer` (default `student`); never accept public `admin` assignment; return the role from `/v1/me`.

    - [ ] Verify Google ID tokens with `google-auth` against configured client IDs; require verified email; create or resolve one Bridge user by verified email and Google subject.
    - [ ] Issue short-lived signed access JWTs and random refresh tokens; store SHA-256 refresh-token hashes only.
    - [ ] Rotate refresh tokens transactionally; revoke a session family on replay; revoke the supplied refresh session at logout.
    - [ ] Replace Supabase JWKS verification with Bridge JWT validation and database user resolution; update `/v1/me` to return Bridge UUID.

    - [ ] State that legacy Supabase accounts/sessions are not migrated and that recovery/verification flows are future work.
    
    ## Completion checks

    - Start the service with no auth settings and confirm `/health` works while auth endpoints fail closed.

    # Bridge API
    
    The Bridge backend is a separate FastAPI service. It owns the API boundary for future marketplace features and uses PostgreSQL for persistence. Supabase Auth owns sign-in; the API verifies bearer access tokens on protected routes.
    
    ## Requirements
    
    - Python 3.11 or newer
    - Docker Desktop or another Docker Compose installation
    - A Supabase project with asymmetric JWT signing keys for local JWKS verification
    
    ## Local setup (PowerShell)
    
    From this directory:
    
    ```powershell
    Copy-Item .env.example .env
    py -3 -m venv .venv
    .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
    python -m pip install --upgrade pip
    python -m pip install -e .
    docker compose up -d db
    docker compose ps
    # Wait until the db service shows healthy before continuing.
    alembic upgrade head
    uvicorn app.main:app --reload
    ```
    
    Set `SUPABASE_URL` in `.env` to the project URL before using protected routes. The API builds its JWKS URL and expected issuer from this URL. `SUPABASE_AUDIENCE` defaults to `authenticated`. Do not put a Supabase service-role key in this API's client-facing configuration.
    
    Supabase's JWKS endpoint serves asymmetric public signing keys. This starter accepts ES256 and RS256 tokens. If the Supabase project uses the legacy HS256 shared-secret signing key, switch the project to an asymmetric signing key before relying on this local JWKS verifier; do not copy a shared signing secret into the app or client. See [Supabase JWT verification](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/jwts).
    
    For `/v1/me`, use an access token from a user session created by a signed-in Supabase Auth client. The Bridge client is not included in this backend starter, so the route becomes usable once a client is configured to sign users in and send its session token.
    
    ## Routes
    
    - `GET /health` - public liveness check; it does not depend on PostgreSQL or Supabase.
    - `GET /v1/me` - returns the verified Supabase user ID; requires `Authorization: Bearer <access-token>`.
    - `/docs` - interactive FastAPI API documentation during local development.
    
    The initial Alembic revision is intentionally empty. No Bridge domain tables or marketplace workflows are implemented yet. Add schema migrations alongside later profile, task, application, and conversation work.
    
    Stop the local database with `docker compose down`. This keeps the named data volume so local records remain available the next time you start it.
    
    ## Configuration
    
    Copy `.env.example` to `.env`. Local database defaults match `compose.yaml`. Keep `.env` private; it is excluded by `.gitignore`. Production hosting, credentials, allowed origins, and operational settings must be configured before deployment.
