    # Bridge API
    
    The Bridge FastAPI service owns authentication and marketplace API access. PostgreSQL stores Bridge users, student onboarding profiles, and refresh sessions. Google is used only to verify Google sign-in credentials; Supabase is not part of runtime authentication.
    
    ## Local setup (PowerShell)
    
    From this directory:
    
    ```powershell
    Copy-Item .env.example .env
    py -3 -m venv .venv
    .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
    python -m pip install --upgrade pip
    python -m pip install -e .
    docker compose up -d db
    docker compose ps
    # Wait until the db service shows healthy before continuing.
    alembic upgrade head
    uvicorn app.main:app --reload
    ```
    
    Set `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` in `.env` to a random value at least 32 characters long. For example, generate one with `python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))"`. Set `GOOGLE_CLIENT_IDS` to a JSON array of the OAuth client IDs used by the mobile app. Google ID tokens must have one of these audiences. Never put a Google client secret or Bridge signing secret in the app.
    
    The auth settings are optional at service startup so `/health` stays available. Auth operations fail closed until the Bridge signing key is configured; Google sign-in also requires Google client IDs. Do not use the sample signing secret in production.
    
    ## Authentication API
    
    - `POST /v1/auth/register` - create an email/password account and issue Bridge tokens. Student role is the default and requires `student_profile`; freelancer accounts omit that field. Public signup cannot assign `admin`.
    - `POST /v1/auth/login` - verify an email/password account and issue Bridge tokens.
    - `POST /v1/auth/google` - verify a Google ID token and create or sign in to a Google-linked Bridge account. For a new student account, include the role and `student_profile`. An email already used by another Bridge account returns `409`; identities are not linked implicitly.
    - `POST /v1/auth/refresh` - rotate a refresh token and return a new token pair.
    - `POST /v1/auth/logout` - revoke the presented refresh token.
    - `GET /v1/me` - return the Bridge user ID, role, and student profile (if present); requires `Authorization: Bearer <access-token>`.
    
    Student registration sends the onboarding fields in `student_profile`:
    
    ```json
    {
      "email": "student@example.com",
      "password": "a-long-password",
      "role": "student",
      "student_profile": {
        "full_name": "Alex Student",
        "key_skills": ["Python", "Research"],
        "experience_level": "beginner",
        "availability_hours_per_week": 12,
        "portfolio_url": "https://example.com/portfolio"
      }
    }
    ```
    
    `key_skills` accepts 1–20 unique tags of up to 60 characters. Experience is `beginner`, `intermediate`, or `advanced`. Weekly availability is 0–168 hours. `portfolio_url` is optional and must use HTTP or HTTPS. A freelancer signup uses `"role": "freelancer"` and omits `student_profile`.
    
    Google sign-in accepts `{ "id_token": "<Google ID token>", "role": "student", "student_profile": { ... } }`. Only the first sign-in creates the Bridge account; follow-up calls identify it by the verified Google subject and keep its stored role/profile. A verified email collision returns `409` rather than merging accounts.
    
    Access tokens are short-lived signed JWTs. Refresh tokens are random opaque values; only their hashes are stored. Reuse of a rotated refresh token revokes its session family. Passwords use Argon2 hashes. Admin roles must be provisioned through a trusted administrative process; no public API can grant them. Email verification, password recovery, and account linking are not implemented yet.
    
    ## Other routes
    
    - `GET /health` - public liveness check; independent of PostgreSQL and auth settings.
    - `/docs` - interactive FastAPI API documentation during local development.
    
    Stop the local database with `docker compose down`. This keeps the named data volume so local records remain available the next time you start it.

## Activity

    $ Get-Content docs/bridge_backend_handoff.md | Select-Object -Skip 15 -First 100; Get-Content docs/mds/bridge_trd.md | Select-Object -First 125
    | Request models | Pydantic | Parse and validate API input and produce OpenAPI schemas. |
    | Database | PostgreSQL | Transactional persistence for profiles, tasks, applications, conversations, and messages. |
    | Database access | SQLAlchemy 2.x | ORM and transaction/session management. |
    | Schema migrations | Alembic | Versioned database schema changes. |
    | Authentication | Supabase Auth | Email/password and Google sign-in; clients send Supabase access JWTs to the API. |
    
    The mobile app calls FastAPI; it does not access marketplace tables directly. The API verifies the Supabase JWT signature, issuer, and expiry using Supabase's published signing keys (JWKS), then applies object-level authorization. Cache signing keys according to library/provider guidance and refresh them safely for key rotation. Do not implement JWT cryptography manually.
    
    Use SQLAlchemy transaction boundaries for multi-row state changes, and PostgreSQL constraints for uniqueness and referential integrity. If the API connects with a privileged database role that bypasses row-level security, RLS is not a substitute for API authorization. Use a least-privileged database role; treat RLS as defense in depth only when the request identity is safely propagated to the database.
    
    Keep one API service for the initial scope. Add a worker, queue, realtime transport, or additional service only when a confirmed product capability requires it. The separate API service requirement is fulfilled by the FastAPI boundary; the prototype does not establish a microservice topology.
    
    ## Screen inventory
    
    | Screen | Visible content and interaction | Backend implication |
    |---|---|---|
    | Splash screen | Bridge branding and loading state. | No domain operation shown; startup may later fetch session/config. |
    | Prototype launch | â€œDouble click to openâ€ prototype card. | Design navigation only; not a product requirement. |
    | Role selection | Student or Freelancer choice; Continue. | Persist selected account mode or use it to route onboarding. |
    | Login / signup | Email, password, Login, Google continuation, sign-up link. | Authenticate through Supabase Auth; signup and recovery details are not shown. |
    | Student onboarding | Full name, skill tags, experience level, weekly availability, portfolio link; save/continue. | Profile create/update with validated skills and optional portfolio URL. |
    | Task details | Title, image, budget, deadline, description, skills; client/task summary. | Read task detail and related client metadata. |
    | Student home | Active task count, pending payment amount, available task cards with budget and detail link. | Dashboard summary plus paginated task discovery; metric definitions remain open. |
    | Apply to task | Task summary, proposal text, portfolio highlights and edit selection. | Create application/proposal linked to task and applicant. |
    | Student profile | Name, avatar, title, experience, portfolio URL, skills, edit profile. | Read/update profile and portfolio references. |
    | Freelancer home | Post New Task; suggested student cards with skill/rating summary and profile action. | Create-task entry point and candidate recommendations/search; ranking behavior is not specified. |
    | Matches | Accepted, pending, and alternative/rejected-looking application cards; open chat; edit action on some cards. | List relationship/application states and create/open a conversation for eligible pairs. |
    | Post a task | Title, description, required skills, budget, deadline, draft/review control. | Create/update task listing; draft/review meaning needs product definition. |
    | Freelancer profile | Name, rating, verified badge, rate/earnings, completion percentage, active tasks. | Read public profile and task/aggregate metrics; verification source is unspecified. |
    | Chat | Conversation history, message composer, send control, project goal/progress card. | Fetch conversation and append messages; project goal/progress behavior needs definition. |
    | Task applicants | Applicant profile/rating/skills with Reject and Accept actions. | List applications and transition their state with authorization and concurrency checks. |
    
    ## User flows
    
    ### Sign in and build a student profile
    
    The user chooses Student, signs in through Supabase Auth using email/password or Google, and completes a profile with name, skills, experience, availability, and portfolio link. The mobile client obtains the Supabase session and sends its access JWT as a bearer token to FastAPI. FastAPI verifies the token and upserts only the caller's profile. The prototype does not show email verification, password reset, or signup fields.
    
    ### Discover and apply to work
    
    Student home presents available tasks and summary metrics. The student opens task details, writes a proposal, selects portfolio highlights, and submits. Recommended application lifecycle: pending â†’ accepted or rejected; withdrawal, editing after submission, and resubmission are not visibly specified.
    
    ### Client posts and reviews a task
    
    Freelancer/client home offers Post New Task. The task form collects title, description, required skills, budget, and deadline. Applicants are shown with profile evidence and Accept/Reject controls. Save as draft versus publication behavior needs clarification.
    
    ### Match and message
    
    Matches groups candidates/application cards into visible status sections, and accepted matches expose Open Chat. A conversation screen shows messages and a project goal/progress card. Recommended: allow conversation access only for an accepted application or another explicitly approved relationship; enforce membership for every read/send.
    
    ## Suggested API surface
    
    **Convention:** Example REST paths and payload names below are proposals. Use `/v1`, JSON, a consistent error envelope, cursor pagination, and ISO-8601 UTC timestamps. FastAPI/Pydantic should define the request and response schemas and publish the OpenAPI contract.
    
    | Method and path | Purpose | Key behavior |
    |---|---|---|
    | Supabase Auth SDK: sign-in, signup, OAuth, refresh, signout | Establish and manage authentication session. | Client uses Supabase Auth; do not duplicate auth endpoints in FastAPI unless a concrete requirement needs a server-owned session layer. |
    | `GET /v1/me` Â· `PATCH /v1/me/profile` | Read identity and edit profile. | Require verified bearer JWT; accept only editable fields. |
    | `GET /v1/tasks` | Browse available tasks. | Filter by skill, budget, deadline; cursor pagination; exclude non-published tasks. |
    | `GET /v1/tasks/{taskId}` | Task detail. | Include only public task and client summary fields. |
    | `POST /v1/tasks` Â· `PATCH /v1/tasks/{taskId}` | Create/edit task. | Owner-only; draft/publish state transition must be agreed. |
    | `POST /v1/tasks/{taskId}/applications` | Submit proposal. | One active application per user/task unless product chooses otherwise; idempotency recommended. |
    | `GET /v1/tasks/{taskId}/applications` | Review applicants. | Task owner only; paginated applicant cards. |
    | `PATCH /v1/applications/{applicationId}` | Accept/reject application. | Owner-only; transactional state transition; handle concurrent decisions. |
    | `GET /v1/me/applications` Â· `GET /v1/me/matches` | Student status lists. | Expose only caller's applications and permitted match states. |
    | `GET /v1/profiles/{profileId}` | Profile detail. | Return public profile fields; redact private account data. |
    | `GET /v1/conversations` Â· `GET /v1/conversations/{id}/messages` | List/read conversations. | Member-only, ordered by sent time, cursor paginated. |
    | `POST /v1/conversations/{id}/messages` | Send message. | Member-only; validate body; persist and return canonical message. |
    
    ## Suggested data model
    
    | Entity | Suggested fields | Notes |
    |---|---|---|
    | User | `id`, Supabase `auth_user_id`, `email` if needed, `status`, `created_at` | Auth credentials remain with Supabase Auth; enforce unique mapping to auth user ID. |
    | Profile | `user_id`, `display_name`, `headline`, `avatar_url`, `experience_level`, `availability_hours_per_week`, `portfolio_url`, `rating_summary`, `verified_at` | Rating and verification calculation/source are unknown. |
    | Skill | `id`, `canonical_name` | Canonical catalog supports consistent filtering. |
    | ProfileSkill | `profile_id`, `skill_id`, proficiency or display order | Only add proficiency if product needs it; UI currently shows tags. |
    | Task | `id`, `owner_user_id`, `title`, `description`, `budget_amount`, `currency`, `deadline_at`, `status`, timestamps | Use decimal or integer minor units plus currency; define draft/review/published states. |
    | TaskSkill | `task_id`, `skill_id` | Many-to-many required skills. |
    | Application | `id`, `task_id`, `applicant_user_id`, `proposal`, `status`, `submitted_at`, `updated_at` | Define duplicate-application policy and enforce it in the database. |
    | ApplicationPortfolioItem | `application_id`, `portfolio_item_id` or URL snapshot | UI lets applicant choose highlights; decide snapshot vs live profile reference. |
    | Conversation | `id`, `task_id` nullable, `created_at` | Tie chat to accepted work if task-scoped conversation is confirmed. |
    | ConversationMember | `conversation_id`, `user_id`, `joined_at` | Unique membership pair supports authorization. |
    | Message | `id`, `conversation_id`, `sender_user_id`, `body`, `sent_at`, `read_at` nullable | Append-only preferred; read receipts are not clearly shown. |
    | TaskProgress / ProjectGoal | `task_id`, goal text/status or progress fields | A progress/goal card appears in chat; exact fields and update controls need definition. |
    
    ## Authentication, authorization, and privacy
    
    - Supabase Auth owns password and Google authentication. The client sends its access token to protected FastAPI routes as `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>`.
    - Validate JWT signature against Supabase signing keys/JWKS and check issuer, expiry, and relevant claims with a maintained JWT library. Never trust a user ID or role supplied only in the request body.
    - Resolve the token subject to the Bridge user/profile record. Enforce object-level authorization: task owner edits tasks and decides applications; applicant accesses only their applications; conversation members alone read/send messages.
    - Treat Student/Freelancer as product modes, not proof of authorization. Whether a user may hold both modes remains open.
    - Return public profile fields through an explicit allowlist. Do not expose email, session data, or private applicant details in public cards.
    - Use a least-privileged Postgres role. If privileged credentials bypass RLS, implement and test authorization in FastAPI; use RLS only as additional defense where the database connection carries safe user context.
    - Rate-limit auth-related and abuse-prone actions at an appropriate edge/API layer. Do not store Supabase secrets in the mobile app or log tokens and sensitive message contents.
    
    ## Validation and consistency
    
    Use Pydantic schemas for input shape and field constraints, with domain-level checks and PostgreSQL constraints for rules that must survive concurrent requests.
    
    # Bridge Technical Requirements Document
    
    **Student talent marketplace | Version 1.1 | 2 November 2026**
    
    ## Purpose
    
    This TRD defines the selected backend technology baseline and technical requirements for Bridge's visible flows: profiles, task listings, proposals, applicant decisions, matches, and text conversations. The selected backend is a separate Python FastAPI service with PostgreSQL persistence. Product rules that the screens do not establish remain open and must be approved before implementation.
    
    ## System boundary
    
    ### In scope
    
    - Supabase Auth integration for the visible email/password and Google sign-in paths.
    - A separately deployed, versioned HTTPS API service that mediates all marketplace data access.
    - Bridge user mapping, user modes, profiles, and skills.
    - Task creation, publication/discovery, details, required skills, and ownership.
    - Proposal submission, applicant listing, and accept/reject transitions.
    - Match/status views and membership-protected text conversation history/send.
    - Summary values shown by the UI only after their source and calculation are defined.
    
    ### Out of scope until approved
    
    Payments/payouts, escrow, ratings algorithms, verification workflows, attachment storage, realtime delivery, recommendations, moderation operations, and administrative tooling.
    
    ## Selected technology architecture
    
    | Concern | Selected technology | Requirement |
    |---|---|---|
    | API service | Python + FastAPI | Own the backend API boundary; expose versioned JSON REST endpoints under `/v1`. |
    | Request validation and schemas | Pydantic | Define request/response models, constraints, and OpenAPI schemas at API boundaries. |
    | Persistence | PostgreSQL | Store transactional marketplace records with foreign keys, unique constraints, and indexes. |
    | ORM/session management | SQLAlchemy 2.x | Use explicit short-lived sessions and transaction boundaries for database work. |
    | Schema migration | Alembic | Version and review schema changes; deploy migrations in a controlled release step. |
    | Identity provider | Supabase Auth | Own sign-in, signup, OAuth, refresh, and signout unless a concrete requirement changes that boundary. |
    | API authorization | FastAPI application layer | Verify token identity, resolve Bridge user, and authorize every object operation. |
    
    ### Request and data path
    
    The mobile client signs in through Supabase Auth, receives an access JWT, then sends that token as a bearer credential to the FastAPI service. FastAPI validates the token, resolves the external subject to a Bridge user, validates the request, checks object-level permissions, calls domain logic, and uses SQLAlchemy to persist records in PostgreSQL. The client does not connect directly to marketplace tables.
    
    Verify JWT signatures using a maintained JWT library and Supabase's published signing keys/JWKS. Check issuer, expiry, and relevant claims. Do not implement cryptographic verification manually or trust a user ID in the request body. Support key rotation according to provider guidance.
    
    Use the least-privileged database role that supports the API's required operations. If database credentials bypass PostgreSQL row-level security, RLS does not protect against application authorization bugs; FastAPI must enforce access rules and authorization tests must cover them. RLS can be added as defense in depth when request identity is safely propagated to the database.
    
    Use one API service for the initial product scope. Do not require microservices, Redis, a task queue, or a separate realtime service until an approved capability or measured operational need requires one.
    
    ## API requirements
    
    All endpoints use JSON, a version prefix, consistent error envelopes, and ISO-8601 UTC timestamps. Collection endpoints support cursor pagination. Mutating requests are safe to retry where feasible; use idempotency keys for application submissions and decisions if clients may retry after timeouts. FastAPI's OpenAPI schema is the API contract source for client integration.
    
    | Method/path or client action | Requirement | Authorization |
    |---|---|---|
    | Supabase Auth SDK: email/password, Google OAuth, refresh, signout | Establish and manage identity session. | Provider-managed; public actions rate-limited. |
    | `GET /v1/me` | Return caller identity and supported user mode(s). | Valid Supabase JWT. |
    | `PATCH /v1/me/profile` | Update allowlisted profile fields. | Authenticated, self only. |
    | `GET /v1/tasks` | Search/browse published tasks with filters and cursor. | Public vs authenticated policy TBD. |
    | `GET /v1/tasks/{id}` | Return public task details. | Public visibility policy TBD. |
    | `POST /v1/tasks` | Create task in agreed initial state. | Task-owner mode. |
    | `PATCH /v1/tasks/{id}` | Edit owned task while allowed by state. | Owner only. |
    | `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/applications` | Submit proposal and portfolio selections. | Student/applicant mode. |
    | `GET /v1/tasks/{id}/applications` | List applicants. | Task owner only. |
    | `PATCH /v1/applications/{id}` | Accept or reject. | Task owner only. |
    | `GET /v1/me/applications` | List caller's applications. | Applicant only. |
    | `GET /v1/me/matches` | List caller's permitted match relationships. | Authenticated. |
    | `GET /v1/conversations/{id}/messages` | Read ordered messages. | Conversation member. |
    | `POST /v1/conversations/{id}/messages` | Append text message. | Conversation member. |
    
    Use a stable error shape with machine-readable code, human-readable message, optional field errors, and request identifier. Do not expose stack traces or internal authorization details. Return 401 for invalid/missing credentials and 403 or a privacy-preserving not-found response for inaccessible resources, consistently.
    
    ## Domain model proposal
    
    | Entity | Core attributes and constraints |
    |---|---|
    | User | ID, unique Supabase auth user ID, optional normalized email, account status, timestamps. |
    | UserMode | User ID and mode (student, task_owner); cardinality awaits product decision. |
    | Profile | User ID, display name, headline, avatar URL, experience level, weekly availability, portfolio URL. Public response uses explicit allowlist. |
    | Skill | Canonical ID and display name. |
    | ProfileSkill | Profile ID, skill ID, optional ordering; unique pair. |
    | Task | ID, owner ID, title, description, amount, currency, deadline, status, timestamps. Store monetary precision explicitly; do not use floating point. |
    | TaskSkill | Task ID, skill ID; unique pair. |
    | Application | ID, task ID, applicant ID, proposal, state, submitted/updated timestamps. Enforce approved duplicate-application rule. |
    | ApplicationPortfolioItem | Application ID plus item reference or submission-time URL snapshot; policy TBD. |
    | Conversation | ID, optional task/application reference, created timestamp. |
    | ConversationMember | Conversation ID, user ID, joined timestamp; unique pair. |
    | Message | ID, conversation ID, sender ID, body, sent timestamp, optional read timestamp. Append-only recommended. |
    
    ## State and consistency requirements
    
    Proposed task states: draft, published, closed, cancelled. Proposed application states: pending, accepted, rejected, optionally withdrawn. Exact states and transitions must be approved.
    
    - Only an owner can publish/edit/close their task, subject to allowed transitions.
    - Only the applicant can create or withdraw their application; only the task owner can decide it.
    - The accept operation executes transactionally with task/application updates. If one hire is allowed, prevent concurrent accepts from creating multiple accepted applications.
    - Conversation creation and access follow an explicit eligibility rule; recommended: accepted relationship or owner-created invite.
    - A caller's `/me` lists never expose another user's private relationships.
    - Aggregates such as active-task count and pending payments derive from an authoritative source and have documented semantics.
    
    ## Validation requirements
    
    - Validate request shape and reject unknown protected fields; enforce maximum text lengths.
    - Validate bearer JWT signature and claims; use generic auth failure responses.
    - Validate skill IDs against the catalog and limit count/duplicates.
    - Enforce valid experience-level enum and non-negative bounded availability.
    - Accept only approved URL schemes for portfolio/avatar URLs; never fetch user-provided URLs server-side as validation.
    - Validate title, description, budget/currency, deadline, and task state at API and domain boundaries.
    - Reject proposals for closed/ineligible tasks; enforce duplicate policy under concurrent requests.
    - Validate message size and non-empty body; safely encode at display boundary and enforce membership at every read/write.
    - Back critical rules with PostgreSQL constraints and transactions, not request validation alone.
    
    ## Security and privacy
    
    - Use TLS for all network traffic. Supabase Auth handles password storage and OAuth flows; the Bridge API must not receive or log raw passwords.
    - Validate Supabase JWTs on the server and map provider subject to an internal user.
    - Apply least privilege and object-level authorization on every task, application, profile, and conversation request.
    - Store only necessary personal data. Exclude email and internal identifiers from public profile payloads unless explicitly approved.
    - Protect Supabase server secrets and database credentials in a managed secret store; never put service-role keys in the client.
    - Do not log bearer tokens, auth credentials, message bodies, or unnecessary personal data.
    - Define account deletion, retention, abuse reporting, moderation, and audit event requirements before production.
    
    ## Reliability, performance, and operations
    
    Numeric SLOs are not in the source and must be set by the owning team. Before launch, define availability, API latency, recovery point/time, data retention, and support hours.
    
    - Use SQLAlchemy/PostgreSQL transactions for state transitions and unique constraints for identity, membership, and duplicate protections.
    - Add request IDs, structured logs, and metrics for errors/latency; audit task/application decisions without recording secrets or message content.
    ✓ • 988ms

    $ Get-Content backend/app/api/v1/me.py; Get-Content backend/app/core/auth.py; Get-Content backend/app/api/dependencies.py; Get-Content backend/app/db/models.py; Get-Content backend/alembic/versions/0002_auth.py; Get-Content backend/app/core/config.py; rg -n -i "supabase|auth_user_id" README.md backend docs --glob '!**/superpowers/specs/2026-11-03-bridge-backend-foundation-design.md' --glob '!**/superpowers/plans/2026-11-03-bridge-backend-foundation.md'
    from typing import Literal
    
    from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends
    from pydantic import AnyHttpUrl, BaseModel, ConfigDict
    from sqlalchemy import select
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.api.dependencies import get_current_user
    from app.db.models import StudentProfile, User
    from app.db.session import get_db
    
    router = APIRouter()
    
    
    class StudentProfileResponse(BaseModel):
        model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)
    
        full_name: str
        key_skills: list[str]
        experience_level: Literal["beginner", "intermediate", "advanced"]
        availability_hours_per_week: int
        portfolio_url: AnyHttpUrl | None
    
    
    class IdentityResponse(BaseModel):
        user_id: str
        role: Literal["admin", "freelancer", "student"]
        student_profile: StudentProfileResponse | None = None
    
    
    @router.get("/me", response_model=IdentityResponse)
    def read_current_user(
        user: User = Depends(get_current_user), db: Session = Depends(get_db)
    ) -> IdentityResponse:
        profile = db.scalar(select(StudentProfile).where(StudentProfile.user_id == user.id))
        return IdentityResponse(
            user_id=str(user.id),
            role=user.role,
            student_profile=StudentProfileResponse.model_validate(profile) if profile else None,
        )
    import hashlib
    import secrets
    from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
    from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    
    import jwt
    from fastapi import HTTPException, status
    from jwt.exceptions import PyJWTError
    from sqlalchemy import select, update
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.core.config import Settings
    from app.db.models import RefreshSession, User
    
    ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(minutes=15)
    REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(days=30)
    JWT_ISSUER = "bridge-api"
    JWT_AUDIENCE = "bridge-api"
    def signing_secret(settings: Settings) -> str:
        secret = settings.auth_jwt_secret
        if not secret or len(secret.encode("utf-8")) < 32:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
                detail="Authentication is not configured",
            )
        return secret
    
    
    def hash_refresh_token(token: str) -> str:
        return hashlib.sha256(token.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    
    
    def issue_tokens(
        db: Session,
        user: User,
        settings: Settings,
        family_id: UUID | None = None,
    ) -> dict[str, str | int]:
        now = datetime.now(UTC)
        access_token = jwt.encode(
            {
                "sub": str(user.id),
                "iss": JWT_ISSUER,
                "aud": JWT_AUDIENCE,
                "iat": now,
                "exp": now + ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
                "jti": str(uuid4()),
                "token_type": "access",
            },
            signing_secret(settings),
            algorithm="HS256",
        )
        refresh_token = secrets.token_urlsafe(48)
        db.add(
            RefreshSession(
                user_id=user.id,
                family_id=family_id or uuid4(),
                token_hash=hash_refresh_token(refresh_token),
                expires_at=now + REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
            )
        )
        return {
            "access_token": access_token,
            "refresh_token": refresh_token,
            "token_type": "bearer",
            "expires_in": int(ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME.total_seconds()),
        }
    
    
    def decode_access_token(token: str, settings: Settings) -> UUID:
        try:
            claims = jwt.decode(
                token,
                signing_secret(settings),
                algorithms=["HS256"],
                issuer=JWT_ISSUER,
                audience=JWT_AUDIENCE,
                options={"require": ["exp", "iat", "iss", "aud", "sub", "jti", "token_type"]},
            )
            if claims.get("token_type") != "access":
                raise PyJWTError("Wrong token type")
            return UUID(claims["sub"])
        except HTTPException:
            raise
        except (PyJWTError, ValueError, TypeError, KeyError) as exc:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            ) from exc
    from fastapi import Depends, HTTPException, status
    from fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials, HTTPBearer
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    
    from app.core.auth import decode_access_token
    from app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
    from app.db.models import User
    from app.db.session import get_db
    
    bearer_scheme = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)
    
    
    def get_current_user(
        credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials | None = Depends(bearer_scheme),
        settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
        db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    ) -> User:
        if credentials is None or credentials.scheme.lower() != "bearer":
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Bearer token required",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            )
    
        user_id = decode_access_token(credentials.credentials, settings)
        user = db.get(User, user_id)
        if user is None:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
                detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
                headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
            )
        return user
    from datetime import UTC, datetime
    from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    
    from sqlalchemy import JSON, CheckConstraint, DateTime, ForeignKey, String, Text, Uuid, func
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped, mapped_column
    
    from app.db.base import Base
    
    
    class User(Base):
        __tablename__ = "users"
        __table_args__ = (CheckConstraint("role IN ('admin', 'freelancer', 'student')", name="ck_users_role"),)
    
        id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid4)
        email: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(320), unique=True)
        password_hash: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(Text, nullable=True)
        google_sub: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(255), unique=True, nullable=True)
        role: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20), default="student", server_default="student")
        created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
            DateTime(timezone=True), default=lambda: datetime.now(UTC), server_default=func.now()
        )
    
    
    class StudentProfile(Base):
        __tablename__ = "student_profiles"
        __table_args__ = (
            CheckConstraint(
                "experience_level IN ('beginner', 'intermediate', 'advanced')",
                name="ck_student_profiles_experience_level",
            ),
            CheckConstraint(
                "availability_hours_per_week BETWEEN 0 AND 168",
                name="ck_student_profiles_availability",
            ),
        )
    
        user_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(
            Uuid, ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), primary_key=True
        )
        full_name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(120))
        key_skills: Mapped[list[str]] = mapped_column(JSON)
        experience_level: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20))
        availability_hours_per_week: Mapped[int] = mapped_column()
        portfolio_url: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(2048), nullable=True)
    
    
    class RefreshSession(Base):
        __tablename__ = "refresh_sessions"
    
        id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid4)
        user_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(
            Uuid, ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), index=True
        )
        family_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, index=True)
        token_hash: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), unique=True)
        expires_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
        revoked_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=True)
        created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
            DateTime(timezone=True), default=lambda: datetime.now(UTC), server_default=func.now()
        )

    # Code Reviewer Prompt Template
    
    Use this template when dispatching a code reviewer subagent.
    
    **Purpose:** Review completed work against requirements and code quality standards before it cascades into more work.
    
    ```
    Subagent (general-purpose):
      description: "Review code changes"
      prompt: |
        You are a Senior Code Reviewer with expertise in software architecture,
        design patterns, and best practices. Your job is to review completed work
        against its plan or requirements and identify issues before they cascade.
    
        ## What Was Implemented
    
        [DESCRIPTION]
    
        ## Requirements / Plan
    
        [PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS]
    
        ## Git Range to Review
    
        **Base:** [BASE_SHA]
        **Head:** [HEAD_SHA]
    
        ```bash
        git diff --stat [BASE_SHA]..[HEAD_SHA]
        git diff [BASE_SHA]..[HEAD_SHA]
        ```
    
        ## The spec is a vision document
    
        The spec says what the software must do. It does not enumerate every
        input, environment, or condition the software will meet. For behavior
        the spec is silent on, judge by what a reasonable person using this
        software would expect: a reasonable person's expectation is a
        requirement, and a spec's silence is not permission. Grade such
        findings by their effect on that person, not by whether the spec
        mentions the trigger.
    
        ## Declined to judge
    
        Before your verdict, list every behavior you considered and set aside
        as outside the plan or spec, one line each, with the reason. The
        executor rules on each line; nothing you set aside is dropped
        silently. An empty list means you set nothing aside.
    
        ## Read-Only Review
    
        Your review is read-only on this checkout. Do not mutate the working tree, the index, HEAD, or branch state in any way. Use tools like `git show`, `git diff`, and `git log` to inspect history. If you need a working copy of a different revision, check it out into a separate temporary directory (e.g. `git worktree add /tmp/review-[SHA] [SHA]`) â€” never move HEAD on this checkout.
    
        ## You Do Not Dispatch Subagents
    
        Do all of this review yourself. Never spawn a subagent to review part
        of the diff, and never spawn another reviewer for a second opinion.
        This process already provides every review seat the work gets; a
        reviewer you spawn duplicates one of them at full cost, and its
        verdict counts for nothing. If the diff feels too large for one
        pass, review it in passes yourself and say so in your report.
    
        ## What to Check
    
        **Plan alignment:**
        - Does the implementation match the plan / requirements?
        - Are deviations justified improvements, or problematic departures?
        - Is all planned functionality present?
    
        **Code quality:**
        - Clean separation of concerns?
        - Proper error handling?
        - Type safety where applicable?
        - DRY without premature abstraction?
        - Edge cases handled?
    
        **Architecture:**
        - Sound design decisions?
        - Reasonable scalability and performance?
        - Security concerns?
        - Integrates cleanly with surrounding code?
    
        **Testing:**
        - Tests verify real behavior, not mocks?
        - Edge cases covered?
        - Integration tests where they matter?
        - All tests passing?
    
        **Production readiness:**
        - Migration strategy if schema changed?
        - Backward compatibility considered?
        - Documentation complete?
        - No obvious bugs?
    
        ## Calibration
    
        Categorize issues by actual severity. Not everything is Critical.
        Acknowledge what was done well before listing issues â€” accurate praise
        helps the implementer trust the rest of the feedback.
    
        If you find significant deviations from the plan, flag them specifically
        so the implementer can confirm whether the deviation was intentional.
        If you find issues with the plan itself rather than the implementation,
        say so.
    
        ## Output Format
    
        ### Strengths
        [What's well done? Be specific.]
    
        ### Issues
    
        #### Critical (Must Fix)
        [Bugs, security issues, data loss risks, broken functionality]
    
        #### Important (Should Fix)
        [Architecture problems, missing features, poor error handling, test gaps]
    
        #### Minor (Nice to Have)
        [Code style, optimization opportunities, documentation polish]
    
        For each issue:
        - File:line reference
        - What's wrong
        - Why it matters
        - How to fix (if not obvious)
    
        ### Recommendations
        [Improvements for code quality, architecture, or process]
    
        ### Assessment
    
        **Ready to merge?** [Yes | No | With fixes]
    
        **Reasoning:** [1-2 sentence technical assessment]
    
        ## Critical Rules
    
        **DO:**
        - Categorize by actual severity
        - Be specific (file:line, not vague)
        - Explain WHY each issue matters
        - Acknowledge strengths
        - Give a clear verdict
    
        **DON'T:**
        - Say "looks good" without checking
        - Mark nitpicks as Critical
        - Give feedback on code you didn't actually read
        - Be vague ("improve error handling")
        - Avoid giving a clear verdict
    ```
    
    **Placeholders:**
    - `[DESCRIPTION]` â€” brief summary of what was built
    - `[PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS]` â€” what it should do (plan file path, task text, or requirements)
    - `[BASE_SHA]` â€” starting commit
    - `[HEAD_SHA]` â€” ending commit
    
    **Reviewer returns:** Strengths, Issues (Critical / Important / Minor), Recommendations, Assessment
    
    ## Example Output
    
    ```
    ### Strengths
    - Clean database schema with proper migrations (db.ts:15-42)
    - Comprehensive test coverage (18 tests, all edge cases)
    - Good error handling with fallbacks (summarizer.ts:85-92)
    
    ### Issues
    
    #### Important
    1. **Missing help text in CLI wrapper**
       - File: index-conversations:1-31
       - Issue: No --help flag, users won't discover --concurrency
       - Fix: Add --help case with usage examples
    
    2. **Date validation missing**
       - File: search.ts:25-27
       - Issue: Invalid dates silently return no results
       - Fix: Validate ISO format, throw error with example
    
    #### Minor
    1. **Progress indicators**
       - File: indexer.ts:130
       - Issue: No "X of Y" counter for long operations
       - Impact: Users don't know how long to wait
    
    ### Recommendations
    - Add progress reporting for user experience
    - Consider config file for excluded projects (portability)
    
    ### Assessment
    
    **Ready to merge: With fixes**
    
    **Reasoning:** Core implementation is solid with good architecture and tests. Important issues (help text, date validation) are easily fixed and don't affect core functionality.
    ```

    +"""Add Bridge accounts and refresh sessions."""
    +
    +from typing import Sequence, Union
    +
    +import sqlalchemy as sa
    +from alembic import op
    +
    +revision: str = "0002_auth"

    +    op.create_table(
    +        "users",
    +        sa.Column("id", sa.Uuid(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("email", sa.String(length=320), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("password_hash", sa.Text(), nullable=True),
    +        sa.Column("google_sub", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True),
    +        sa.Column("role", sa.String(length=20), server_default="student", nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("created_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=sa.func.now(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.CheckConstraint("role IN ('admin', 'freelancer', 'student')", name="ck_users_role"),
    +        sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("id"),
    +        sa.UniqueConstraint("email"),
    +        sa.UniqueConstraint("google_sub"),
    +    )
    +    op.create_table(
    +        "student_profiles",
    +        sa.Column("user_id", sa.Uuid(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("full_name", sa.String(length=120), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("key_skills", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("experience_level", sa.String(length=20), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("availability_hours_per_week", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("portfolio_url", sa.String(length=2048), nullable=True),
    +        sa.CheckConstraint(
    +            "experience_level IN ('beginner', 'intermediate', 'advanced')",
    +            name="ck_student_profiles_experience_level",
    +        ),
    +        sa.CheckConstraint(
    +            "availability_hours_per_week BETWEEN 0 AND 168",
    +            name="ck_student_profiles_availability",
    +        ),
    +        sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["user_id"], ["users.id"], ondelete="CASCADE"),
    +        sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("user_id"),
    +    )
    +    op.create_table(
    +        "refresh_sessions",
    +        sa.Column("id", sa.Uuid(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("user_id", sa.Uuid(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("family_id", sa.Uuid(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("token_hash", sa.String(length=64), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("expires_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False),
    +        sa.Column("revoked_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=True),
    +        sa.Column("created_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=sa.func.now(), nullable=False),
    +        sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["user_id"], ["users.id"], ondelete="CASCADE"),
    +        sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("id"),
    +        sa.UniqueConstraint("token_hash"),
    +    )
    +    op.create_index("ix_refresh_sessions_family_id", "refresh_sessions", ["family_id"])
    +    op.create_index("ix_refresh_sessions_user_id", "refresh_sessions", ["user_warning: in the working copy of 'backend/app/api/v1/auth.py', LF will be replaced by CRLF the next time Git touches it
    id"])
    +
    +
    +def downgrade() -> None:
    +    op.drop_index("ix_refresh_sessions_user_id", table_name="refresh_sessions")
    +    op.drop_index("ix_refresh_sessions_family_id", table_name="refresh_sessions")

    diff --git a/backend/app/api/v1/auth.py b/backend/app/api/v1/auth.py
    new file mode 100644
    index 0000000..6c97702
    --- /dev/null
    +++ b/backend/app/api/v1/auth.py
    @@ -0,0 +1,285 @@
    +from datetime import UTC, datetime
    +from typing import Annotated, Literal
    +
    +from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, Response, status
    +from google.auth import exceptions as google_exceptions
    +from google.auth.transport.requests import Request as GoogleRequest
    +from google.oauth2 import id_token as google_id_token
    +from pydantic import AnyHttpUrl, BaseModel, EmailStr, Field, StringConstraints, UrlConstraints, model_validator
    +from pwdlib import PasswordHash
    +from sqlalchemy import select, update
    +from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError
    +from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    +
    +from app.core.auth import hash_refresh_token, issue_tokens, signing_secret
    +from app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
    +from app.db.models import RefreshSession, StudentProfile, User
    +from app.db.session import get_db
    +
    +router = APIRouter(prefix="/auth", tags=["authentication"])
    +password_hasher = PasswordHash.recommended()
    +dummy_password_hash = password_hasher.hash("bridge-invalid-login-placeholder")
    +RoleChoice = Literal["student", "freelancer"]
    +Skill = Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=1, max_length=60)]
    +
    +
    +class StudentProfileInput(BaseModel):
    +    full_name: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=2, max_length=120)]
    +    key_skills: list[Skill] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20)
    +    experience_level: Literal["beginner", "intermediate", "advanced"]
    +    availability_hours_per_week: int = Field(ge=0, le=168)
    +    portfolio_url: Annotated[AnyHttpUrl, UrlConstraints(max_length=2048)] | None = None
    +
    +    @model_validator(mode="after")
    +    def skills_are_unique(self):
    +        if len({skill.casefold() for skill in self.key_skills}) != len(self.key_skills):
    +            raise ValueError("key_skills must be unique")
    +        return self
    +
    +
    +class RegisterRequest(BaseModel):
    +    email: EmailStr
    +    password: str = Field(min_length=8, max_length=128)
    +    role: RoleChoice = "student"
    +    student_profile: StudentProfileInput | None = None
    +
    +    @model_validator(mode="after")
    +    def require_student_profile(self):
    +        if self.role == "student" and self.student_profile is None:
    +            raise ValueError("student_profile is required for student accounts")
    +        if self.role == "freelancer" and self.student_profile is not None:
    +            raise ValueError("student_profile is only valid for student accounts")
    +        return self
    +
    +
    +class LoginRequest(BaseModel):
    +    email: EmailStr
    +    password: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=128)
    +
    +
    +class GoogleSignInRequest(BaseModel):
    +    id_token: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=8192)
    +    role: RoleChoice = "student"
    +    student_profile: StudentProfileInput | None = None
    +
    +
    +class RefreshRequest(BaseModel):
    +    refresh_token: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=512)
    +
    +
    +class TokenResponse(BaseModel):
    +    access_token: str
    +    refresh_token: str
    +    token_type: Literal["bearer"] = "bearer"
    +    expires_in: int
    +
    +
    +def normalize_email(email: str) -> str:
    +    return email.strip().casefold()
    +
    +
    +def add_student_profile(db: Session, user: User, profile: StudentProfileInput | None) -> None:
    +    if profile is not None:
    +        db.add(
    +            StudentProfile(
    +                user_id=user.id,
    +                full_name=profile.full_name,
    +                key_skills=profile.key_skills,
    +                experience_level=profile.experience_level,
    +                availability_hours_per_week=profile.availability_hours_per_week,
    +                portfolio_url=str(profile.portfolio_url) if profile.portfolio_url else None,
    +            )
    +        )
    +
    +
    +def unauthorized() -> HTTPException:
    +    return HTTPException(
    +        status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
    +        detail="Invalid credentials",
    +        headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
    +    )
    +
    +
    +def configured_google_audiences(settings: Settings) -> list[str]:
    +    audiences = [value.strip() for value in settings.google_client_ids if value.strip()]
    +    if not audiences:
    +        raise HTTPException(
    +            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
    +            detail="Google sign-in is not configured",
    +        )
    +    return audiences
    +
    +
    +def verify_google_token(token: str, settings: Settings) -> dict:
    +    audiences = configured_google_audiences(settings)
    +    try:
    +        claims = google_id_token.verify_oauth2_token(
    +            token,
    +            GoogleRequest(),
    +            audience=audiences[0] if len(audiences) == 1 else audiences,
    +        )
    +    except ValueError as exc:
    +        raise unauthorized() from exc
    +    except google_exceptions.GoogleAuthError as exc:
    +        raise HTTPException(
    +            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
    +            detail="Google authentication service unavailable",
    +        ) from exc
    +    if (
    +        not isinstance(claims.get("sub"), str)
    +        or not claims["sub"]
    +        or not isinstance(claims.get("email"), str)
    +        or claims.get("email_verified") is not True
    +    ):
    +        raise unauthorized()
    +    return claims
    +
    +
    +@router.post("/register", response_model=TokenResponse, status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED)
    +def register(
    +    request: RegisterRequest,
    +    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    +    settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    +) -> dict:
    +    signing_secret(settings)
    +    email = normalize_email(str(request.email))
    +    if db.scalar(select(User.id).where(User.email == email)) is not None:
    +        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists")
    +
    +    user = User(
    +        email=email,
    +        password_hash=password_hasher.hash(request.password),
    +        role=request.role,
    +    )
    +    db.add(user)
    +    try:
    +        db.flush()
    +        add_student_profile(db, user, request.student_profile)
    +        tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings)
    +        db.commit()
    +    except IntegrityError as exc:
    +        db.rollback()
    +        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists") from exc
    +    return tokens
    +
    +
    +@router.post("/login", response_model=TokenResponse)
    +def login(
    +    request: LoginRequest,
    +    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    +    settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    +) -> dict:
    +    signing_secret(settings)
    +    user = db.scalar(select(User).where(User.email == normalize_email(str(request.email))))
    +    encoded_password = user.password_hash if user and user.password_hash else dummy_password_hash
    +    if not password_hasher.verify(request.password, encoded_password) or user is None:
    +        raise unauthorized()
    +    tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings)
    +    db.commit()
    +    return tokens
    +
    +
    +@router.post("/google", response_model=TokenResponse)
    +def google_sign_in(
    +    request: GoogleSignInRequest,
    +    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    +    settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    +) -> dict:
    +    signing_secret(settings)
    +    claims = verify_google_token(request.id_token, settings)
    +    email = normalize_email(claims["email"])
    +    user = db.scalar(select(User).where(User.google_sub == claims["sub"]))
    +    if user is not None:
    +        if user.email != email:
    +            existing_email = db.scalar(select(User.id).where(User.email == email))
    +            if existing_email is not None and existing_email != user.id:
    +                raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists")
    +            user.email = email
    +    else:
    +        user = db.scalar(select(User).where(User.email == email))
    +        if user is not None:
    +            raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Account already exists")
    +        if user is None:
    +            if request.role == "student" and request.student_profile is None:
    +                raise HTTPException(
    +                    status_code=status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY,
    +                    detail="student_profile is required for new student accounts",
    +                )
    +            if request.role == "freelancer" and request.student_profile is not None:
    +                raise HTTPException(
    +                    status_code=status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY,
    +                    detail="student_profile is only valid for student accounts",
    +                )
    +            user = User(email=email, google_sub=claims["sub"], role=request.role)
    +            db.add(user)
    +            try:
    +                db.flush()
    +                add_student_profile(db, user, request.student_profile)
    +            except IntegrityError as exc:
    +                db.rollback()
    +                raise HTTPException(
    +                    status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT,
    +                    detail="Account already exists",
    +                ) from exc
    +    tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings)
    +    db.commit()
    +    return tokens
    +
    +
    +@router.post("/refresh", response_model=TokenResponse)
    +def refresh(
    +    request: RefreshRequest,
    +    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    +    settings: Settings = Depends(get_settings),
    +) -> dict:
    +    signing_secret(settings)
    +    session = db.scalar(
    +        select(RefreshSession)
    +        .where(RefreshSession.token_hash == hash_refresh_token(request.refresh_token))
    +        .with_for_update()
    +    )
    +    if session is None:
    +        raise unauthorized()
    +
    +    now = datetime.now(UTC)
    +    if session.revoked_at is not None:
    +        db.execute(
    +            update(RefreshSession)
    +            .where(
    +                RefreshSession.family_id == session.family_id,
    +                RefreshSession.revoked_at.is_(None),
    +            )
    +            .values(revoked_at=now)
    +        )
    +        db.commit()
    +        raise unauthorized()
    +    if session.expires_at <= now:
    +        session.revoked_at = now
    +        db.commit()
    +        raise unauthorized()
    +
    +    user = db.get(User, session.user_id)
    +    if user is None:
    +        session.revoked_at = now
    +        db.commit()
    +        raise unauthorized()
    +
    +    family_id = session.family_id
    +    session.revoked_at = now
    +    tokens = issue_tokens(db, user, settings, family_id=family_id)
    +    db.commit()
    +    return tokens
    +
    +
    +@router.post("/logout", status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)
    +def logout(request: RefreshRequest, db: Session = Depends(get_db)) -> Response:
    +    db.execute(
    +        update(RefreshSession)
    +        .where(
    +            RefreshSession.token_hash == hash_refresh_token(request.refresh_token),
    +            RefreshSession.revoked_at.is_(None),
    +        )
    +        .values(revoked_at=datetime.now(UTC))
    +    )
    +    db.commit()
    +    return Response(status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)

    diff --git a/backend/app/core/auth.py b/backend/app/core/auth.py
    new file mode 100644
    index 0000000..9e196e4
    --- /dev/null
    +++ b/backend/app/core/auth.py
    @@ -0,0 +1,91 @@
    +import hashlib
    +import secrets
    +from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
    +from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    +
    +import jwt
    +from fastapi import HTTPException, status
    +from jwt.exceptions import PyJWTError
    +from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
    +
    +from app.core.config import Settings
    +from app.db.models import RefreshSession, User
    +
    +ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(minutes=15)
    +REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME = timedelta(days=30)
    +JWT_ISSUER = "bridge-api"
    +JWT_AUDIENCE = "bridge-api"
    +
    +
    +def signing_secret(settings: Settings) -> str:
    +    secret = settings.auth_jwt_secret
    +    if not secret or len(secret.encode("utf-8")) < 32:
    +        raise HTTPException(
    +            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
    +            detail="Authentication is not configured",
    +        )
    +    return secret
    +
    +
    +def hash_refresh_token(token: str) -> str:
    +    return hashlib.sha256(token.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    +
    +
    +def issue_tokens(
    +    db: Session,
    +    user: User,
    +    settings: Settings,
    +    family_id: UUID | None = None,
    +) -> dict[str, str | int]:
    +    now = datetime.now(UTC)
    +    access_token = jwt.encode(
    +        {
    +            "sub": str(user.id),
    +            "iss": JWT_ISSUER,
    +            "aud": JWT_AUDIENCE,
    +            "iat": now,
    +            "exp": now + ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
    +            "jti": str(uuid4()),
    +            "token_type": "access",
    +        },
    +        signing_secret(settings),
    +        algorithm="HS256",
    +    )
    +    refresh_token = secrets.token_urlsafe(48)
    +    db.add(
    +        RefreshSession(
    +            user_id=user.id,
    +            family_id=family_id or uuid4(),
    +            token_hash=hash_refresh_token(refresh_token),
    +            expires_at=now + REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME,
    +        )
    +    )
    +    return {
    +        "access_token": access_token,
    +        "refresh_token": refresh_token,
    +        "token_type": "bearer",
    +        "expires_in": int(ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME.total_seconds()),
    +    }
    +
    +
    +def decode_access_token(token: str, settings: Settings) -> UUID:
    +    try:
    +        claims = jwt.decode(
    +            token,
    +            signing_secret(settings),
    +            algorithms=["HS256"],
    +            issuer=JWT_ISSUER,
    +            audience=JWT_AUDIENCE,
    +            options={"require": ["exp", "iat", "iss", "aud", "sub", "jti", "token_type"]},
    +        )
    +        if claims.get("token_type") != "access":
    +            raise PyJWTError("Wrong token type")
    +        return UUID(claims["sub"])
    +    except HTTPException:
    +        raise
    +    except (PyJWTError, ValueError, TypeError, KeyError) as exc:
    +        raise HTTPException(
    +            status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
    +            detail="Invalid or expired bearer token",
    +            headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"},
    +        ) from exc

    diff --git a/backend/app/db/models.py b/backend/app/db/models.py
    new file mode 100644
    index 0000000..b00a469
    --- /dev/null
    +++ b/backend/app/db/models.py
    @@ -0,0 +1,60 @@
    +from datetime import UTC, datetime
    +from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    +
    +from sqlalchemy import JSON, CheckConstraint, DateTime, ForeignKey, String, Text, Uuid, func
    +from sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped, mapped_column
    +
    +from app.db.base import Base
    +
    +
    +class User(Base):
    +    __tablename__ = "users"
    +    __table_args__ = (CheckConstraint("role IN ('admin', 'freelancer', 'student')", name="ck_users_role"),)
    +
    +    id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid4)
    +    email: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(320), unique=True)
    +    password_hash: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(Text, nullable=True)
    +    google_sub: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(255), unique=True, nullable=True)
    +    role: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20), default="student", server_default="student")
    +    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
    +        DateTime(timezone=True), default=lambda: datetime.now(UTC), server_default=func.now()
    +    )
    +
    +
    +class StudentProfile(Base):
    +    __tablename__ = "student_profiles"
    +    __table_args__ = (
    +        CheckConstraint(
    +            "experience_level IN ('beginner', 'intermediate', 'advanced')",
    +            name="ck_student_profiles_experience_level",
    +        ),
    +        CheckConstraint(
    +            "availability_hours_per_week BETWEEN 0 AND 168",
    +            name="ck_student_profiles_availability",
    +        ),
    +    )
    +
    +    user_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(
    +        Uuid, ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), primary_key=True
    +    )
    +    full_name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(120))
    +    key_skills: Mapped[list[str]] = mapped_column(JSON)
    +    experience_level: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20))
    +    availability_hours_per_week: Mapped[int] = mapped_column()
    +    portfolio_url: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(2048), nullable=True)
    +
    +
    +class RefreshSession(Base):
    +    __tablename__ = "refresh_sessions"
    +
    +    id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid4)
    +    user_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(
    +        Uuid, ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), index=True
    +    )
    +    family_id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(Uuid, index=True)
    +    token_hash: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), unique=True)
    +    expires_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
    +    revoked_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=True)
    +    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(
    +        DateTime(timezone=True), default=lambda: datetime.now(UTC), server_default=func.now()
    +    )

## Assistant

The reviewer also found a refresh replay race: concurrent refreshes could let a replay revoke older tokens while missing a just-created token. I’m serializing refresh and replay handling on the account row so each rotation sees the previous transaction’s session state.

## Assistant

Implemented Bridge-owned authentication with email/password and Google sign-in, Bridge-issued access and refresh tokens, and PostgreSQL-backed accounts and sessions.

- Added registration, login, Google token exchange, refresh, and logout routes in [auth.py](/D:/roohan/bridge/backend/app/api/v1/auth.py). Refresh tokens are rotated and stored as hashes; replay or logout revokes that session family.
- Added `admin`, `freelancer`, and `student` roles. Public signup can choose student or freelancer; it cannot grant admin.
- Student signup stores full name, skills, experience level, weekly availability, and optional portfolio URL. `/v1/me` returns the role and student profile.
- Added the reversible auth schema migration in [0002_auth.py](/D:/roohan/bridge/backend/alembic/versions/0002_auth.py), replaced Supabase verification, and updated the project docs. No Supabase accounts need migration.
- Google and password accounts aren’t linked automatically by matching email; collisions return `409`.

A code review found and helped fix four security issues. Syntax compilation, dependency-file parsing, and whitespace checks passed.

Skipped: automated tests and database migration execution. The local dependency install was blocked by restricted PyPI access, so install the declared dependencies and run `alembic upgrade head` before using the API. Email verification and recovery remain unimplemented.

## User

what commands do i have to run

## Assistant

From PowerShell, in `D:\roohan\bridge\backend`:

```powershell
Copy-Item .env.example .env
```

Edit `.env` and set:

- `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` to a random secret of at least 32 characters. Generate one with:
  ```powershell
  python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))"
  ```
- `GOOGLE_CLIENT_IDS` to a JSON array containing your Google OAuth client ID(s).

Then install dependencies, start PostgreSQL, apply the migration, and run the API:

```powershell
py -3 -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
python -m pip install --upgrade pip
python -m pip install -e .
docker compose up -d db
alembic upgrade head
uvicorn app.main:app --reload
```

The API will be at `http://localhost:8000`; interactive docs are at `http://localhost:8000/docs`.

    # Bridge
    
    Bridge is a student talent marketplace concept. The product screens describe student profiles, task discovery and applications, task-owner applicant review, matches, and conversations.
    
    ## Project contents
    
    - [`backend/`](backend/) - separate Python FastAPI service with PostgreSQL, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, Bridge-owned authentication, and Google sign-in.
    - [`docs/`](docs/) - product, business, technical, and engineering handoff documents.
    
    ## Start the backend
    
    See the [backend setup guide](backend/README.md) for prerequisites, local PostgreSQL setup, configuration, and run commands.
    
    The backend provides email/password and Google sign-in APIs, Bridge access and refresh tokens, student onboarding profiles, a public health route, and a protected identity route. Marketplace workflows are not implemented yet.
    
    ## Key documents
    
    - [Product Requirements Document](docs/pdfs/bridge_prd.pdf)
    - [Business Requirements Document](docs/pdfs/bridge_brd.pdf)
    - [Technical Requirements Document](docs/pdfs/bridge_trd.pdf)
    - [Backend Engineering Handoff](docs/pdfs/bridge_backend_handoff.pdf)
    - [Backend Engineering Handoff (Markdown)](docs/bridge_backend_handoff.md)
    - [Backend Foundation Implementation Plan](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-03-bridge-backend-foundation.md)

    # Bridge-owned Authentication Implementation Plan
    
    > **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
    
    **Goal:** Replace Supabase token verification with Bridge-owned email/password and Google sign-in APIs backed by PostgreSQL.
    
    **Architecture:** Add Bridge user and refresh-session tables, issue short-lived Bridge JWT access tokens, and store only hashes of rotating opaque refresh tokens. Google clients exchange a Google ID token at the API; the API verifies it and issues the same Bridge tokens.
    
    **Tech Stack:** FastAPI, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, PostgreSQL, PyJWT, Argon2 password hashing, Google ID token verification.
    
    **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-bridge-owned-auth-design.md`
    
    ## Global Constraints
    
    - Never store plaintext passwords or refresh tokens.
    - Do not trust decoded Google claims without signature verification and configured audience validation.
    - Keep `/health` independent of database and auth configuration.
    - Do not add password recovery, email verification, MFA, or client implementation.
    
    ## Review Focus
    
    - Missing or short Bridge signing secret: auth operations fail closed with a clear server configuration error.
    - Malformed, expired, wrong-audience, or unverified-email Google ID token: Google exchange is rejected.
    - Duplicate normalized emails or Google subjects: do not create or cross-link duplicate accounts.
    - Invalid student profile fields: reject out-of-range skills, experience, availability, and URL inputs before persistence.
    - Refresh token rotation race or replay: only one rotation succeeds and reuse revokes the session family.
    - Existing Supabase token: protected routes reject it after the provider verifier is removed.
    
    ---
    
    ### Task 1: Add auth persistence and settings
    
    **Files:**
    - Create: `backend/app/db/models.py`
    - Modify: `backend/app/core/config.py`
    - Modify: `backend/alembic/env.py`
    - Create: `backend/alembic/versions/0002_auth.py`
    - Modify: `backend/.env.example`
    - Modify: `backend/pyproject.toml`
    
    **Interfaces:**
    - `User`: UUID `id`, normalized unique `email`, nullable `password_hash`, nullable unique `google_sub`, role constrained to `admin|freelancer|student` (default `student`), creation timestamp.
    - `StudentProfile`: one-to-one with a student user; full name, 1â€“20 unique skill tags, beginner/intermediate/advanced experience, weekly availability hours (0â€“168), optional HTTP(S) portfolio URL.
    - `RefreshSession`: UUID `id`, `user_id`, `family_id`, unique token hash, expiry, optional revoked timestamp, creation timestamp.
    - Settings: optional `auth_jwt_secret` and `google_client_ids`; auth calls validate required values at use time so health remains independent.
    
    - [ ] Define the user, student-profile, and refresh-session models; constrain user role and profile experience/availability; register them with `Base.metadata` for Alembic.
    - [ ] Add reversible migration `0002_auth` creating all three tables, uniqueness constraints, and foreign keys.
    - [ ] Add auth configuration and `google-auth` plus Argon2 password hashing dependencies; remove no runtime config until the auth verifier is switched in Task 2.
    - [ ] Replace Supabase sample settings with Bridge signing-secret and Google client-ID settings.
    
    ### Task 2: Implement Bridge token verification and auth routes
    
    **Files:**
    - Create: `backend/app/core/auth.py`
    - Create: `backend/app/api/v1/auth.py`
    - Modify: `backend/app/api/dependencies.py`
    - Modify: `backend/app/api/v1/me.py`
    - Modify: `backend/app/api/v1/router.py`
    
    **Interfaces:**
    - `get_current_user(...) -> User`: validate Bridge bearer JWT and resolve its UUID subject to an active database user.
    - Auth routes: `POST /auth/register`, `/auth/login`, `/auth/google`, `/auth/refresh`, `/auth/logout`.
    - Token response: `access_token`, `refresh_token`, `token_type="bearer"`, `expires_in`.
    - Registration and first Google sign-in accept `role=student|freelancer` (default `student`); never accept public `admin` assignment; return the role from `/v1/me`.
    - Student signup requires full name, unique key skills, experience level, weekly availability hours, and optional portfolio URL.
    
    - [ ] Implement Argon2 password hashing, normalized-email registration/login, and generic invalid-credential responses.
    - [ ] Verify Google ID tokens with `google-auth` against configured client IDs; require verified email; create or resolve one Bridge user by Google subject and reject email collisions rather than linking identities implicitly.
    - [ ] Require student profile data for new student registrations and first-time Google sign-in; do not mutate existing user role or profile during Google sign-in.
    - [ ] Issue short-lived signed access JWTs and random refresh tokens; store SHA-256 refresh-token hashes only.
    - [ ] Rotate refresh tokens transactionally; revoke a session family on replay or logout.
    - [ ] Replace Supabase JWKS verification with Bridge JWT validation and database user resolution; update `/v1/me` to return Bridge UUID.
    - [ ] Mount auth routes under the v1 router and validate required signing-key and Google-audience configuration at relevant endpoints.
    
    ### Task 3: Update project documentation
    
    **Files:**
    - Modify: `backend/README.md`
    - Modify: `README.md`
    - Modify: `docs/bridge_backend_handoff.md`
    - Modify: `docs/mds/bridge_backend_handoff.md`
    - Modify: `docs/mds/bridge_trd.md`
    
    - [ ] Document database migration, local Bridge signing secret, Google client IDs, auth request/response shapes, and how clients send Bridge access tokens.
    - [ ] Remove current-state claims that Supabase owns sign-in or that Supabase JWTs protect the API; leave dated historical design artifacts unchanged.
    - [ ] State that no Supabase accounts need migration and that recovery/verification flows are future work.
    
    ## Completion checks
    
    - Apply and roll back the Alembic auth migration against a local PostgreSQL database.
    - Start the service with no auth settings and confirm `/health` works while auth endpoints fail closed.
    - Review the route schemas and documentation for all five auth operations, student signup profile fields, and Bridge-only bearer token behavior.

    # Bridge
    
    Bridge is a student talent marketplace concept. The product screens describe student profiles, task discovery and applications, task-owner applicant review, matches, and conversations.
    
    ## Project contents
    
    - [`backend/`](backend/) - separate Python FastAPI service with PostgreSQL, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, Bridge-owned authentication, and Google sign-in.

    # Bridge API
    
    Bridge is a student talent marketplace. The FastAPI service, PostgreSQL setup, and Python project now live at the repository root; product and engineering documents are in [`docs/`](docs/).
    
    The API owns email/password accounts and sessions. Google sign-in exchanges a Google ID token for Bridge tokens. It stores user roles and student signup profiles. Supabase is not used for runtime authentication.
    
    ## Local setup (PowerShell)
    
    From the repository root:
    
    ```powershell
    Copy-Item .env.example .env
    py -3 -m venv .venv
    .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
    python -m pip install --upgrade pip
    python -m pip install -e .
    ```
    
    Set `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` in `.env` to a random value of at least 32 characters. Generate one with:
    
    ```powershell
    python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))"
    ```
    
    Set `GOOGLE_CLIENT_IDS` to a JSON array of the mobile app's Google OAuth client IDs. Keep the signing secret on the server; Google ID-token verification does not need a Google client secret.
    
    Then start PostgreSQL, apply migrations, and run the API:
    
    ```powershell
    docker compose up -d db
    docker compose ps
    # Wait until the db service is healthy.
    alembic upgrade head
    uvicorn app.main:app --reload
    ```
    
    The Compose project name is kept as `backend` so an existing local database volume continues to be used after moving the Compose file. `GET /health` works without auth configuration. Auth routes fail closed until their required settings are present.
    
    ## Authentication routes
    
    - `POST /v1/auth/register` - create an email/password account; `student` is the default role and needs a `student_profile`. Freelancer signup uses `"role": "freelancer"` without that field. Public signup cannot grant `admin`.
    - `POST /v1/auth/login` - verify email and password.
    - `POST /v1/auth/google` - exchange a Google ID token for Bridge tokens. New student accounts include the role and profile; existing accounts keep their saved role/profile. Matching an existing email returns `409`; identities aren’t linked implicitly.
    - `POST /v1/auth/refresh` - rotate a refresh token. Reuse revokes that session family.
    - `POST /v1/auth/logout` - revoke the presented refresh token’s entire session family.
    - `GET /v1/me` - return the user ID, role, and student profile, if present. Send the access token as `Authorization: Bearer <access-token>`.
    
    Student signup includes full name, 1–20 unique skill tags (up to 60 characters each), experience (`beginner`, `intermediate`, or `advanced`), weekly availability (0–168 hours), and an optional HTTP(S) portfolio URL. Passwords use Argon2 hashes; refresh tokens are stored only as hashes. Email verification, password recovery, and account linking aren’t implemented. Admin accounts must be provisioned through a trusted process.
    
    ## Project documents
    
    - [Product Requirements Document](docs/pdfs/bridge_prd.pdf)
    - [Business Requirements Document](docs/pdfs/bridge_brd.pdf)
    - [Technical Requirements Document](docs/pdfs/bridge_trd.pdf)
    - [Backend Engineering Handoff](docs/pdfs/bridge_backend_handoff.pdf)
    - [Backend Engineering Handoff (Markdown)](docs/bridge_backend_handoff.md)
    - [Bridge-owned authentication design](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-bridge-owned-auth-design.md)
    - [Bridge-owned authentication implementation plan](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-bridge-owned-auth.md)
